On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 21 v1.0

# The Mathematical Form of Field-Law Generation in Geomorphic Concretized Fields

1\. Purpose

### 1.1  Use and Central Question

　This paper is a Mathematical Form (principal form, S ↦ M; the specification for Mathematical Forms in Canon Chapter 1, Part Three, Chapter Two of the main book *On Self-Manifestation* v0.7.8). Its object of study is the geomorphic concretized field. Terrain elevation, slope and cross-sections are observables; what we study is how mountain hillslopes, footslopes, channels, creeks and gullies, lakeshores and lake basins, together with their material structure, become organized in the processes of flow, transport, deposition and climate, and how this organization in turn governs later activity. Use: to give exact representations of geomorphic states, permitted histories, dynamics and observation, so that “how a law is generated” becomes expressible, runnable, solvable, decidable and readable back on a declared fluvial-lacustrine geomorphic model, and to write the conditions of holding and failure that can be derived as predictions that measurement can refute.  
　The central question: which Trace-Responses were at first retained separately, how did they later join into a set and form tendency, and how, written back again and again by actual activity, did they finally bring a relation that did not hold at first to acquire governing force over later activity and Law qualification?  
　Basis of reading: “Historical dynamics forms tendency; the carrying-out of tendency is activity; activity and tendency organizing (that is, organizing the set) is transformation, and it transforms according to tendency, as water seeks low ground.” (\[S9\], Section 2.1, F2.) Ontology: “A Sedimentation-Field is: the Trace-Response set that signals leave in a medium, together with the mode of organization of this set, transforming under the action of historical dynamics, and its activity.” In the geomorphic object of this paper, “as water seeks low ground” is made concrete through the seven steps by the flow-division law (Lemma 21-1); the quotation does not take the place of proof.

### 1.2  Two Layers of Results

　(1) How climate laws remain in landforms: starting from candidate regularities of seasonal, temperature and wet–dry variation, we derive under what conditions they leave which Trace-Responses and what organization of them, which joint readings can distinguish different climate histories, and which can only narrow the range of candidates (Section 10). The entry point is two histories with the same initial terrain and the same cumulative precipitation but a different temporal order.  
　(2) How geomorphic organization generates new laws: starting from an identified Trace-Response set, we derive under what formative history it acquires stable governance over later activity, which key Trace-Responses and connections bear it, and which shallowing or disconnection makes this relation fail; we compare before and after formation by the same criterion and test its actual bearing by intervention (Sections 8, 9, 11).

### 1.3  Strength and the Three Columns

　Column A, the model column: theorems, finite verifications and finite exhaustive adjudications on the declared model 𝔐21 (the local baseline) and the extended model 𝔐21⁺ (Section 11), which may give 1, 0 or ⊥. Column B, the prediction column: conditional conclusions and breachable predictions for real landforms, written as a priori protocols (Section 13). Column C, the reality column: empirical certification; there is no item of empirical certification at all, and the gaps are predictions awaiting testing, not adjudicated negative. The three columns stand apart: A is not read as B, nor B as C; ⊥ is not read as 0. Theoretical derivation, in-model proof, literature facts and empirical certification are booked separately. Parameters are dimensionless constructed values, not calibrations of real objects; literature facts are cited only to the level checked (12.9), and each source supports only the propositions it states and does not endorse this paper's generalizations.

2\. Core Thesis

### 2.1  Load-Bearing Proposition

　On the declared fluvial-lacustrine geomorphic model, determine the time at which the carrying organization of the selected main-route relation first attains the structural positive end, and give the range over which it can be read back as a law under explicitly stated conditions.  
　This paper's answer is stated conditionally (8.5, Theorem 21-17): on 𝔐21 the candidate relation R*\_θ(P) (route P carries the main throughflow and the main channel-exit sediment transport for both kinds of inflow) does not hold on the initial terrain; in history H1 the writes of the first 4 high flows make the selected carrying organization R = {u\_P, c\_P} Established, the subsequent re-traversal obtains segment-level Ω\_W support, and at the end of macro-step 10 it first becomes the structural positive end (Established ∧ DynamicRole ∧ ΩSupport); under the three host correspondences (Corr-E, Corr-D, Corr-Ω) and an explicitly declared connecting step of holding-tendency semantics (SC\_Hold: the selected current capacity corresponds to ReplayPotential, the current qualification content of the source text's “holding-tendency”), Theorem 4 of *The Necessity Form of Law\* gives a forward read-back of Law, with range limited to the ends of macro-steps 10–16 of H1. The negative end of Law before this is not adjudicated.

### 2.2  Components of the Conclusion

　1\. Model (Section 4): the mechanism laws are taken as rational representatives of established forms, with exact rational arithmetic; on event beats the budget closes exactly before booking, and across beats the separate accounts are identical position by position (C5b, C5c).  
　2\. Trace-Responses and elements (5.1, 5.2): under the unified frame Φ8, u\_P, c\_P, m\_P and the footslope deposit on the opposite route u\_Q (reverse type) obtain element certificates, E = {u\_P, c\_P, m\_P, u\_Q}; the opposite channel reach c\_Q is an activity residue; the hillslope regolith deficit is a Trace-Response carrier but is hidden from the probe (supply saturation); lake-basin sediment is a record-type Trace-Response that is not read; neither of these two enters E.  
　3\. Organization (5.3): the organization hypergraph on E is a single component and contains co-participation edges of {u\_P, c\_P}; the lake basin is a sink only in the process graph; the necessary condition for joint formation (Theorem 21-2).  
　4\. Tendency and write-back (5.4, 5.6): the positive gully-mouth–channel-reach loop and the negative mouth-bar loop; the single-event competition threshold (Theorem 21-5); the end-of-macro-step upper bound on the mouth bar (Proposition 21-6) and a sufficient condition for no backfilling (Corollary 21-6a).  
　5\. Candidate dominance (6.3, 8.4): the in-model dominance predicate Dom\_N of the candidate law is exhaustively decidable on the declared finite class (Theorem 21-4); under protocol P8, Dom\_8 of H1 first holds at the end of macro-step 4; under protocol P∞ there are invariant-box certificates at the ends of macro-steps 6 and 8; the truncation–challenge and shift protocols locate the formative part.  
　6\. Order dependence (8.3, Theorem 21-7): H1 and H2 have the same cumulative inflow, upper limit on stripping and actual stripped amount (301/64); the former generates candidate dominance, while the latter buries and erases the old gully-mouth Trace-Response, after which the two routes are symmetric position by position.  
　7\. Law-formation read-back (8.5, 9.2): the structural positive end of the selected R first occurs at the end of macro-step 10 of H1; what bears the duty is the historical increment relative to the rest reference, not the preset old groove; the current holding-tendency has two complementary kinds of evidence (the finite-horizon capacity Cap\_8 under perturbation, and the two-entry cycle without external maintenance); for the forward read-back of Law see 2.1.  
　8\. Intervention and failure (9.1–9.3): erasing the gully mouth or the channel-reach incision makes candidate dominance lost but recoverable; reducing capacity by burying the mouth is not a disconnection; a true disconnection leaves neither route with candidate dominance; reverting the both-position increments and cutting off flow through route P, both before and after the first structural positive end, keep it from appearing or make it exit; the mouth bar does not belong to the selected R; the recovery of physical throughflow and the recovery of re-traversal qualification occur at two different times.  
　9\. Climate trace-leaving (Section 10): with ample sediment supply the 12 seasonal sequences fall into only 3 classes (Theorem 21-9(A)); with limited supply, terrain separates 12, the event layers 11, the annual layers and cumulative totals 9, and the hillslope 4; exact class counts and separation margins are reported separately.  
　10\. The seventh step (5.7): on the same T = {u\_P, c\_P} and the same frame Φ8, TraceSet, Part and HDyn each hold, and in model mode Fld = 1; at beat 16, the organization graph of T alone, the Part of the same beat and the difference from the rest reference form the bearing chain.  
　11\. River–lake feedback and avulsion succession (Section 11): the lake level simultaneously changes the backwater reads and writes of both routes and opens outflow at the lake outlet; with the lake level clamped, the original route is kept; with feedback on, the rising lake level makes the original route aggrade and crevasse, and the new channel N, after a transition period, takes over the same frozen duty; under lake-level feedback, the three sediment destinations change the times of opening and succession.

3.2  Clauses of the Law Group Form (main book v0.7.8, Law Chapters; cited only by the clauses read, without changing their conclusions)  
　The necessary form of *The General Form of Law* (Law Group Form, Chapter 1 v2.6): C0 ∧ C2 ∧ H1 ∧ H2 ∧ H3 ⇒ \[Law\_F,t(L, I) ⇒ ∃R(Established\_F,t(R) ∧ Govern\_F,t(L; R, I))\], Govern ⇔ DynamicRole ∧ ΩSupport; Established consists of CurrentCarrier, CarrierRealizes, CarrierNonInstant and sedimentary carrying; DynamicRole has existential polarity, while CarrierRealizes, CarrierNonInstant and ΩSupport have coverage polarity; ΩSupport is adjudicated by Ω\_W of Definition 12 of Field Chapter 1 v2.7 (1 when all three items hold: the re-traversal record within the declared window W reaches the declared criterion, the connection of the Gully-Line followed is maintained within W, and every sub-segment of W has re-traversal events along the same class of path).  
　The sufficient direction of *The Necessity Form of Law* v1.4 (Theorem 4): ∃R\[Established ∧ Govern\] ⇒ Law, Conditional on SC\_Hold; it carries no C0, C2 or H1–H3 and does not pass through the formal-layer read-back of *The Mathematical Form of Law* (*The Master Map of the Law Group Form*, 3.3, 6.2). SC\_Hold: for the same R, when Established, DynamicRole and ReplayHist (Ω\_W = 1, with W ending at t) hold, ReplayPotential, the current qualification content of the source text's “holding-tendency”, holds; one way to supply it is to set up a separate current-capacity quantity (declaring its input conditions, maintenance conditions, evaluation horizon and criterion) and prove that it is given by the antecedents (*The Necessity Form of Law*, Section 6). The history of Ω\_W belongs to the segment and is not marked by re-entry conditions (scope statement (1) of proof (iii-a) of the same paper).  
3.3  Time Window and Pre-Existing Terrain  
　The scope of study is an interval in which the terrain has already formed and the new influence of crustal movement within the chosen time window is negligible; new tectonic influence does not enter the model. The pre-existing terrain (the initial state of the model) takes part as a common initial condition in constraining later activity: at the footslope there is already a shallow incision of an old gully mouth, u\_P = 1/4, the other segments are 0, and the hillslope regolith is h = 4 (h = 0 in the supply-limited case). What we study is how, on this basis, climate processes such as the turn of the seasons, temperature change and wet–dry alternation shape and rewrite landforms. Coverage: mountain hillslopes, footslopes and channels, creeks and gullies, lakeshores, lake basins and their connections; riverbed deposition and flow division are included as a set of instances of fluvial landforms.

### 3.4  Marks, Evidence Tags and Usage

　\[proved\] a proof is given in this paper; \[verified\] finite verification or finite exhaustive adjudication by the appendix programs (numbers Cxx refer to verification items of Appendix B.2, Xxx to those of B.3 and Lxx to those of B.4; a finite exhaustive enumeration on the declared finite class is an adjudication, while other verifications cover only the listed generated domain and do not replace proof); \[declared\] a declared item (frozen before adjudication, not proved by this paper); \[pending\] a derivation or piece of evidence not yet completed. Counterexamples are numbered F21-n. Evidence tags follow \[17\] and \[18\]: \[Fact\] \[Published model\] \[Mechanism hypothesis\] \[Derivation\] \[Conditional conclusion\] \[Prediction\] \[Insufficient\] \[Representational choice\].  
　Usage: whatever refers to the Trace-Response set is written “organization” (mode of organization); “Group Form” refers only to the papers of this series. Reading of *The Ontological Structure Diagram of the Sedimentation-Field* v0.4: the dynamic re-action D′ᵢ connects directly to the Trace-Response set Tᵢ₊₁ of the next moment.  
　The four readings of “governance” are kept apart: candidate dominance (Dom\_N, 6.3); the structural positive end (Established ∧ DynamicRole ∧ ΩSupport on the same R, 8.5); current capacity (Cap\_8 and the maintenance-free cycle, 8.5(4)); the forward read-back of Law (with conditions, Theorem 21-17). None of the four stands in for another.  
4\. Object, States, Permitted Histories and Mechanism Laws

### 4.1  Object and Time Window \[declared\]

　Host instance χ21 = (P, L, B, W). P: on the pre-existing terrain, the hillslope regolith, the footslope (the gentle-slope belt at the fan apex), two alternative routes into the lake (P and Q, each consisting of three segments in series: the gully-mouth segment at the footslope, the downstream channel reach and the lakeshore mouth bar) and the lake basin. L: the node–edge geomorphic hierarchy (one Structural Position per segment). B: from the hillslope catchment boundary to the lake basin; the out-of-boundary signals are the hillslope inflow Q, the upper limit on hillslope stripping s\_max, the lake level ℓ, winter freeze-thaw (beat F) and dry periods (beat R). W: a finite window counted in “beats”; the new influence of crustal movement does not enter the model (time-window declaration). The pre-existing terrain (initial state init) is a common initial condition: u\_P = 1/4 means that the footslope already has a shallow incision of an old gully mouth, the other segments are 0, and the hillslope regolith is h = 4 (or h = 0 in the supply-limited case).  
　Coverage: mountain hillslopes (h); footslopes and channels, creeks and gullies (u\_j, c\_j); lakeshores (mouth bars m\_j and their reworking by lake waves); the lake basin (V, with its event layers and annual layers); the connections between geomorphic units are borne by flow division (the connection of water) and transport (the connection of sediment). Riverbed deposition and flow division are included as instances of fluvial landforms: backwater deposition in the channel reach and mouth-bar blockage in this model are a node–edge reduction of the mechanism of backwater-controlled delta avulsion (Column B of Section 13 aligns it with the flume objects of Ganti 2016 and Chadwick 2022).  
4.2  Structural Positions, Signals and Mechanism Laws  
　Structural Positions (8): u\_P, c\_P, m\_P, u\_Q, c\_Q, m\_Q, h, V. Beats: event beats e ∈ {B (high flow: Q = 4, s\_max = 1/4, ℓ = 0), S (low flow: Q = 9/4, s\_max = 3/8, ℓ = 0)}; rest beat R; freeze-thaw beat F. Parameters (dimensionless constructed values): g0 = 1, h0 = 1, Se = S0 = 3/4, Lb = 4, ke = 1/2, kt = 2, ωt = ωc = 1, λ = 1/2, β = 1/2, κ = 1/16, μ = 1/4, wd = 1/64, ft = 1/8; clipping bounds u, c ∈ \[−1/2, 3\], m ∈ \[0, 2\], h ∈ \[0, 4\]; grid G = 256 (C0 output).  
　L1  Conductance \[Representational choice; rational representative of hydraulic geometry\]: segment conductances a = g0(h0 + u)², b = g0(h0 + c)² (quadratic in the flow depth h0 + u, as a rational representative of the Manning-type “conveyance grows superlinearly with flow depth”; the exponent 2 is a representational choice); a route in series has g = ab/(a + b) (Kirchhoff series conductance).  
　L2  Flow division (as water seeks low ground) \[Derivation, Kirchhoff node equation\]: head φ at the footslope node, outlet sill height equal to the route's own mouth-bar height m\_j, Q\_j = g\_j(φ − m\_j), ΣQ\_j = Q, no backflow (Q\_j truncated to \[0, Q\]). Declared disconnection switch (9.1): when route j is in the disconnection set, g\_j = 0 and all inflow enters the other route. Closed form (working domain g\_P, g\_Q \> 0): Q\_P = g\_P(Q + g\_Q(m\_Q − m\_P))/(g\_P + g\_Q), truncated to \[0, Q\] (Lemma 21-1; C3a: equal at every grid point to the direct solution of the node equation); the single-route closure branch (some g\_j = 0) does not use this formula and is adjudicated separately by the branches of route: the closed route has Q\_j = 0 and the other route Q = the entire inflow (if both are closed, both are 0).  
　L3  Scour \[Published form: detachment-limited, excess shear stress or stream power, Howard 1994 (bibliographic record); “the erosion rate is proportional to the excess over the threshold” as stated at Eq. 15 of the main text of Tucker and Slingerland 1997; the stream-power form of Eq. 1 of Whipple and Tucker 1999 with n = 1\]: E = ke·(ω − ωc)⁺, ω = Q\_j·S.  
　L4  Transport and budget \[Published form: threshold capacity-limited sediment transport (a rational representative of the modified Bagnold formula described by Tucker and Slingerland 1997); Exner mass conservation\]: cap = kt·(ω − ωt)⁺. Gully mouth: incoming sediment load = σ·Q\_j/Q, outgoing sediment load₁ = min(cap, load + E), bed change Δu = load₁ − load = min(cap − load, E) (when undersaturated, scour is limited by the smaller of the remaining capacity and the detachment rate; when overloaded, the excess load − cap is deposited entirely at the footslope: complete deposition on the gentle footslope is a representational choice, based on the statement in the main text of Ganti 2014 that avulsions on fans occur where the slope drops abruptly). Channel reach: d = cap₂ − load₁, Δc = min(E₂, d⁺) − λ(−d)⁺, channel-exit sediment out = load₁ + Δc.  
　L5  Backwater \[Published mechanism: non-uniform flow in the backwater zone causes deposition (main text of Ganti 2016, abstract of Chadwick 2022); here a representational choice of node–edge reduction\]: channel slope S\_c = (S0 − (m\_j + ℓ)/Lb)⁺; the mouth bar and the lake level raise the outlet base level and reduce the channel slope.  
　L6  Lake-mouth progradation and entry into the lake: the β fraction of out is added to the route's own mouth bar (m\_j += β·out), and the remaining (1 − β)·out enters the lake basin (V +=). Declared sediment-destination switch (9.1, used only for route P): β\_P is the bar-retention rate; the difference not retained on the bar is either reassigned to the lake basin or exported from the system. The blocking action of the mouth bar is realized through the sill height of L2 (a reduction of the conclusion of Edmonds and Slingerland 2007 that “pressure on the upstream side of the bar makes the flow go around the bar”).  
　L7  Hillslope sediment supply \[Representational choice: switching between supply limit and transport limit\]: the hillslope stripping in one event is σ = min(h, s\_max), entering the footslopes of the two routes in proportion to their inflow shares, and h decreases by σ. When h ≥ s\_max it is transport-limited (supply saturation); when h \< s\_max it is supply-limited.  
　L8  Rest and freeze-thaw \[Published form: a single-notch reduction of linear hillslope diffusion (Eq. 2 of Tucker and Slingerland 1997): depressions are refilled by the factor (1 − κ); lake waves rework the mouth bar by the factor (1 − μ); wetting–drying weathering and freeze-thaw produce regolith (Hales and Roering 2007, bibliographic record; the mechanism only as a candidate)\]: R: u and c each decrease by the fraction κ, m decreases by the fraction μ, h increases by wd; F: h increases by ft (the ground surface freezes and the lake surface ices over; everything else is unchanged—the declared seasonal representation).  
　Post-processing (position-wise, the LP of D18-1 of \[18\]): clipping and gridding floor(x·G)/G, executed by jhc\_core.Host.step of the appendix of \[17\].  
　The sediment budget is booked on two levels. (1) On event beats, before booking: hillslope stripping σ + Σ\_j(Δu\_j + Δc\_j) = Σ\_j Δm\_j + ΔV (+ export, if declared), holding exactly (C5b). Proof: Σ\_j load\_j = σ; out\_j = load\_j + Δu\_j + Δc\_j; Δm\_j + ΔV\_j (+ export\_j) = out\_j. ∎ (2) Across beats: diffusive infilling on rest beats (u, c toward 0; the material is declared as out-of-boundary exchange with the hillslopes on both sides), lake-wave reworking (mouth-bar material is declared as dispersed into the far field of the lake and is not counted in V), wetting–drying weathering and freeze-thaw supply (h, out-of-boundary production), and the clipping residual of post-processing (deposition below the burial limit is cut off, declared as lateral loss at the footslope) and the gridding residual, each kept in separate accounts by process; final value − initial value = the sum of the bookings of all processes + clipping residual + gridding residual, exactly, position by position (C5c). Over the whole of H1: hillslope stripping 4.7031, bar retention and lake-basin sediment input 7.0388 each, removal from the mouth bars by lake-wave reworking 6.7441, diffusive infilling 7.7761, no clipping residual; over the whole of H2: burial-limit overflow at the gully mouths u\_P 0.4075, u\_Q 0.4480 (values to four decimal places; exact values in the C5 output). \[S14\] U14-5: this model is candidate material for that item, and its formal read-back follows the clauses of \[S14\]; this model does not belong to 𝒦\_g.

### 4.3  Relations, Activity and Readings

　Activity (D18-7 of \[18\]): on event beats y = (Q\_P, Q\_Q, S\_P, S\_Q), where S\_j = out\_j is the channel-exit sediment transport (the actual amount of sediment through the outlet cross-section at the end of the channel; its β fraction stays on the route's own mouth bar and the rest enters the lake basin, L6); on rest and freeze-thaw beats y = 0 (C1e). Tendency Φ\_σ: σ ↦ y on the signal class {B, S} (D18-6 of \[18\]).  
　Main relation R\*\_θ(j) (θ = 2/3) \[declared\]: when event e arrives, route j carries the main throughflow (Q\_j ≥ θQ) and carries the main channel-exit sediment transport (S\_j \> 0 and S\_j ≥ θ(S\_P + S\_Q)). This is the conjunctive reading of “repeatedly carries the main throughflow” and “repeatedly carries the main sediment transport”; readings take only the actual flow division and the actual channel-exit sediment amounts of event beats (the same set of readings is used for all comparisons, and the readings do not change between before and after formation). When the total channel exit is positive and the actual lake input of every route is its channel exit multiplied by the same positive proportionality coefficient, the channel-exit shares equal the actual lake-input shares (the default, in which every route takes the same β \< 1, is such a case); in general the two are computed separately, and individual readings may coincide (when the sediment-destination switch of 9.1 changes β\_P, the two are read separately).

### 4.4  Permitted Histories \[declared\]

　Beats: event beats B, S, rest beat R, freeze-thaw beat F. Macro-step: one event beat followed by r = 3 rest beats; in the adjudication protocols one declared perturbation may be applied at the end of a macro-step (6.3). Read first, then step, at the end of a macro-step: at the end of each macro-step the relation is read once under both kinds of inflow, and only then is the next event beat taken.  
　Histories used (generated verbatim by the appendix programs): formative history HF8 = (B·R³)⁸; H1 = (B·R³)⁸(S·R³)⁸; H2 = (S·R³)⁸(B·R³)⁸; H1m = (B·R³)⁸(S·R³·B·R³)⁴; H★ = (B·R³)⁸(S·R³·B·R³)⁸; the family of seasonal sequences: the 12 permutations of the yearly freeze-thaw block F⁴, high-flow block B·R³ and two low-flow blocks S·R³, each repeated for 4 years (10.1); the common subsequent drive (B·R³·S·R³·S·R³)¹⁰; the inflow class 𝓒\_K of the adjudication protocols (events taken from {B, S}, with no more than K − 1 consecutive low flows); the history family 𝓗\_10 (all {B, S} event sequences of length ≤ 10 starting from the initial terrain). The fixed input of the extended model is (F⁴·B·R³·S·R³·S·R³)¹⁵ (11.4).  
　H1 and H2 have the same cumulative inflow 50, cumulative upper limit on stripping 5, actual stripped amount 301/64 and number of rest beats (C0c): they are the entry contrast of “the same initial terrain, the same cumulative precipitation, a different temporal order”.

5\. Unfolding the Seven-Step Research Procedure  
5.1  Step One: Find the Trace-Responses (Unified Frame Φ8)  
5.1.1  Signal, Medium, Change; Retained Changes and the Activity of the Moment  
　Signals: hillslope inflow of water and sediment, lake level, winter freeze-thaw, dry periods. Media: hillslope regolith, footslope alluvium, channel-reach bed surface, lakeshore sand bars, lake-basin floor. Changes left: incision or aggradation of the gully-mouth and channel-reach bed surfaces, growth of the mouth bars, deficit of the hillslope regolith, accumulation of lake-basin sediment. The throughflows Q\_j and sediment transports S\_j of the two routes exist only on event beats and are 0 on rest beats (C1e); activity that varies with the Trace-Responses does not thereby become a Trace-Response (Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\]). \[Derivation\]

### 5.1.2  Unified Frame \[declared\]

　The three-way split, element certificates, organization hypergraph and seventh-step verdicts must be evaluated on the same set, formative history and window. This paper uses frame Φ8 throughout: formative history HF8 = (B·R³)⁸; frame state w8, its end state (= the end of macro-step 8 of H1); as reference history, the pure-rest history of the same length (the reference history of Definition 1 of \[17\]: signal segments replaced by rest); τ\_act = 1, τ\_W = 12, ε = 1/100. The three-way split (Definition 2′), element certificates (Definitions 3′, 4′), organization hypergraph (Definition 5′) and TraceSet, Part, HDyn (Definitions 6′, 7′) are all evaluated on Φ8.  
　Frame state w8 = {u\_P = 555/256, c\_P = 419/256, m\_P = 129/256, u\_Q = −15/256, c\_Q = 13/256, m\_Q = 0, h = 19/8, V = 319/64}; reference state = {u\_P = 1/256, others 0, h = 4, V = 0} (C1 output).  
5.1.3  Three-Way Split (Definition 2′ of \[17\]) \[verified\] (C1a–C1d)  
　u\_P: signal-formation difference 277/128; after supply cut-off from w8, complete self-retention under rest for 63 beats (the tail evidence is a sub-threshold invariant set) → Trace-Response carrier. c\_P: 419/256; 59 beats → Trace-Response carrier. m\_P: 129/256; 13 beats ≥ τ\_W → Trace-Response carrier. u\_Q (reverse type): −15/256; under gridding it stalls in the grid band (5.1.4) and has only a lower bound (within 400 beats it does not return to within ε) → Trace-Response carrier (a lower bound ≥ τ\_W suffices for a positive determination). c\_Q: 13/256; 11 beats \< τ\_W, complete → activity residue. m\_Q: difference from the reference state 0, not a candidate.  
　h (hillslope regolith deficit): a medium with drift, oriented toward deficit (o = −1), with the reference history as ground state; difference −13/8, retained for 104 beats (after both histories reach the upper limit hmax the difference is 0 and stays 0) → Trace-Response carrier. V (lake-basin sediment): difference 319/64, unchanged under rest → a permanently retained record. The formation and retention of both are adjudicated under the same reference (C1c; the depth test of h takes the reference history as baseline, not a zero baseline).  
　Frame dependence (C1d): under another frame Φ4 (HF4 = (B·R³)⁴) the mouth bar, at 85/256, has complete self-retention for 11 beats and is an activity residue. \[Conditional conclusion\] The three-way split of the mouth bar varies with amplitude and the declared window; elements are issued only under Φ8, and the conclusions under Φ4 are listed separately, not merged.  
5.1.4  Bearing and Retention Scales; Gridding Bias  
　The retention of the gully mouth and the channel reach is borne by the hillslope diffusive infilling rate κ, that of the mouth bar by the lake-wave reworking rate μ, and that of the hillslope regolith by the weathering supply and the upper limit hmax; the lake basin has no shallowing process in this model. Gridding bias: under floor gridding the rest map sends −n/G (1 ≤ n \< 1/κ = 16) to itself (C2f)—footslope deposits smaller than 16/256 never decay under gridding semantics, which is a representational distortion; u\_Q = −15/256 lies exactly within this band, its “lower bound only” retention verdict is affected by this, and this is registered under U21-8.  
5.2  Step Two: Single Trace-Responses and Element Certificates  
5.2.1  Element Certificates (Definitions 3′, 4′ of \[17\]; the same frame as 5.1.3) \[verified: exhaustive over the own-position grid domain\]  
　The other carriers are at the trace-free ground state (all 0), δ = o·(w − w⁰):  
　u\_P: probe B, read-out Q\_P, K2 strict order isomorphism, K3 (write condition B) repetition does not erase; c\_P likewise; m\_P: read-out Q\_P (the higher the mouth bar, the smaller Q\_P; the participation effect is strictly increasing), K3 passes; negative values of u\_Q: read-out Q\_P, write condition S, K2 and K3 pass; c\_Q: K2 and K3 pass, but its three-way split is activity residue.  
　Element certificate set E = {u\_P, c\_P, m\_P, u\_Q} (C2a).  
　h does not enter E (C2b): under the deficit orientation, the participation effect for probe B and read-out S\_P is nonzero only when h \< s\_max(B); most of the deficit in the frame domain (h ≥ s\_max) is hidden from the probe, and K2 does not hold (Definition 3′ of \[17\]: the probe must be changed or a probe class declared; no probe class has been declared separately). \[Conditional conclusion\] The hillslope regolith is a Trace-Response carrier but, in the supply-saturated regime, not an element of bearable depth; this has the same source as “supply saturation masks the freeze-thaw sequence” in Section 10.  
　V does not enter E (C2c): V is not read by any read-out law or write law, its participation effect for every probe and read-out is 0, and K2 cannot hold. V is kept as a record position of the climate trace-leaving layer and as a sink of the process graph (5.3.3).  
5.2.2  Deepening, Shallowing, Saturation, Addition, Elimination \[verified\]  
　Deepening: the initial old gully mouth u\_P = 1/4 deepens to 73/128 in one high flow, and the opposite u\_Q goes from 0 to 45/256 (C2d), Φ1a. Shallowing: diffusive infilling under rest (Φ2), burial of the mouth by footslope deposition in a low flow under flow division (C2g: one low flow changes u\_Q from 0 to −11/64), backwater deposition in the channel reach (Theorem 21-5(B)), lake-wave reworking of the mouth bar. Addition: footslope deposition is a reverse-type Trace-Response newly added at the position of the original gully mouth. Saturation: the base-level limit, the burial limit, the mouth-bar upper limit, supply saturation s\_max (C2h).  
　Theorem 21-3 (Elimination and gridding bias) \[proved\]  (1) Under floor gridding the rest map of a positive value n/G is floor((1 − κ)n)/G ≤ (n − 1)/G, so from n/G it reaches 0 in at most n beats; u\_P from 1 reaches 0 at beat 53 (C2e). Under the original mechanism it only approaches 0 asymptotically. (2) The rest map of a negative value −n/G is −⌈(1 − κ)n⌉/G; when κn \< 1, ⌈(1 − κ)n⌉ = n and −n/G is a fixed point (for the declared κ = 1/16, n = 1, …, 15, C2f). ∎ Note: negative values do not stall at −1/G; every point within −15/G is a fixed point.

### 5.3  Step Three: Organization of the Set

5.3.1  Flow Division and “As Water Seeks Low Ground”  
　Lemma 21-1 \[proved\]  The formulas of this lemma that involve division are stated only on the working domain g\_P, g\_Q \> 0 (outside the declared disconnection switch, g\_j ≥ 1/8 \> 0 always holds, C7Lh). (1) The closed form of L2 equals the solution of the node equation (C3a). (2) In the interior ∂Q\_P/∂g\_P = g\_Q(φ − m\_P)/(g\_P + g\_Q) \> 0, ∂Q\_P/∂g\_Q = −g\_P(φ − m\_Q)/(g\_P + g\_Q) \< 0, ∂Q\_P/∂m\_P = −g\_Pg\_Q/(g\_P + g\_Q) \< 0, and Q\_P is constant where truncated; g\_j is strictly increasing in u\_j and c\_j. Hence Q\_P is nondecreasing in u\_P, c\_P, m\_Q and nonincreasing in u\_Q, c\_Q, m\_P (C3b). ∎ (3) Single-route closure branch: when g\_P = 0, Q\_P = 0 and Q\_Q = Q; when g\_Q = 0 and g\_P \> 0, Q\_P = Q; on this branch the monotonicity in the positions of the unclosed route is that of a constant and is not given by the derivative formulas of (2).  
5.3.2  Necessary Condition for Joint Formation  
　Theorem 21-2 \[proved\]  Assume the working domain g\_P, g\_Q \> 0, event inflow Q, Δm = m\_Q − m\_P. If Q\_P ≥ θQ, then (1) m\_P − m\_Q \< (1 − θ)Q/g\_Q; (2) g\_P ≥ g\* := θQg\_Q/((1 − θ)Q + g\_QΔm); (3) min(a\_P, b\_P) ≥ g*: the gully mouth and the channel reach must both reach the same lower bound. Proof: when Q\_P ≥ θQ \> 0 the value before truncation is ≥ θQ, i.e. g\_P(Q + g\_QΔm) ≥ θQ(g\_P + g\_Q); rearranging, g\_P((1 − θ)Q + g\_QΔm) ≥ θQg\_Q \> 0, which gives (1) and (2); the series formula g = ab/(a + b) ≤ min(a, b) gives (3). ∎ (C3c.) Corollary 21-2a: the upper bound in (1) tightens as Q decreases, so under the same mouth-bar difference the low flow is the first to lose the main throughflow (after the disconnection of 9.1, route Q bears only for the high flow, an instance of this). Witnesses (C3d): a single segment incised down to the base-level limit gives the share 0.6531 \< 2/3; both segments incised by 1/2 each give the share 0.6923. The single-route closure branch is adjudicated separately: when g\_Q = 0 and g\_P \> 0, Q\_P = Q, the throughflow condition holds automatically and (1)–(3) do not apply (here the closure of route Q is a declared disconnection, not caused by the Trace-Responses of P); when g\_P = 0, Q\_P = 0 and R*\_θ(P) does not hold.  
5.3.3  Organization Hypergraph (Definition 5′ of \[17\], only on the element certificate set E) \[verified\]  
　Exact enumeration over all nonempty subsets of E = {u\_P, c\_P, m\_P, u\_Q} × depth multiples {0, 1/2, 2}, at frame state w8, with probes B and S: 16 hyperedges, one component {c\_P, m\_P, u\_P, u\_Q} (C3e). Co-participation hyperedges: Q\_P, Q\_Q and S\_P are jointly determined by the four positions, S\_Q by {c\_P, u\_P} (the organization edge of {u\_P, c\_P}); history-condition hyperedges: the mouth-bar write mouth@m\_P, the channel-reach write scour\_c@c\_P and the two gully-mouth writes scour\_e@u\_P and scour\_e@u\_Q are each changed by the settings of other positions—Kirchhoff conservation makes interventions between subsets globally nonzero.  
　Process graph (an informal hypergraph, C3f): when V is added, V serves only as the write target of a history-condition hyperedge (basin@V) and appears in no co-participation hyperedge and in no setting subset of a write to another position—a sink that records but is not read. The element set is not constructed directly by \|w\| \> ε, and V is not listed in the formal hypergraph.  
5.4  Step Four: Historical Dynamics Forms Tendency

### 5.4.1  What Bears the Tendency

　The tendency Φ\_σ (D18-6 of \[18\]) is the activity propensity that the formed Trace-Responses and their organization give on the signal class {B, S}; its bearers are the conductances of L1 (u\_j, c\_j), the outlet sill heights of L2 (m\_j), and the hillslope sediment supply of L7 (h). Tendency precedes activity: the same state gives different activity for B and for S (C4a).  
　The tendency of three states (C4; Q\_P/Q, S\_P, S\_Q):  
　　Initial terrain: B → 0.5495, 0.7857, 0.4643; S → 0.5495, 0, 0 (the low flow only drops the hillslope sediment at the footslope; none enters the lake).  
　　End of macro-step 8 of H1 (frame state w8): B → 0.8378, 1.5117, 0; S → 0.7948, 0.3502, 0.  
　　End of macro-step 8 of H2: B → 0.5, 0.625, 0.625; S → 0.5, 0, 0.  
　“Historical dynamics forms tendency” is concrete here: the tendency of frame state w8 directs both kinds of inflow toward P and makes even the low flow enter the lake through P; the tendency of H2 favors neither route, and the low flow still does not enter the lake. The Trace-Response sets of the three states were written by their respective histories (5.6), and the same signal is carried out as different activity in the three states.  
5.4.2  Tendency Received at the Own Position and Loop Direction (D18-13 and Theorem 18-5(3) of \[18\]) \[verified\]  
　Frame state w8, inflow B: for u\_P the direction of ψ (Q\_P with u\_P) is +, the direction of the write (Δu with Q\_P) is +, and the loop is +; c\_P is likewise +; for m\_P the direction of ψ is − (the higher the mouth bar, the smaller Q\_P), the direction of the write (mouth-bar increment with channel-exit sediment, channel-exit sediment with Q\_P) is +, and the loop is − (C4b). Reading: the gully mouth and the channel reach form a positive loop of continued writing (the deeper, the more water; the more water, the more incision: the rich get richer), and the mouth bar a self-limiting negative loop (the more water, the more sediment sent to the mouth; the higher the mouth bar, the more water lost). This is exactly the formal bearing of what the central question asks: “one activity may deepen the entrance and at the same time leave at the lake mouth a deposit that impedes the next throughflow.”  
5.4.3  Single-Event Competition Threshold and Compatibility Gate  
　Theorem 21-5 \[proved\]  Let the event inflow be Q, the own-route throughflow Q\_P \> 0, the hillslope stripping σ \> 0, and kt·Se \> σ/Q.  
　(A) Mouth burial: Δu \< 0 if and only if Q\_P \< Q\*\_e := ωt/(Se − σ/(Q·kt)).  
　(B) Channel-reach backfilling: assume load₁ \> 0 and write m*₀ := Lb\[S0 − (ωt + load₁/kt)/Q\_P\] for the threshold that does not include the lake level. Δc \< 0 if and only if m\_P + ℓ \> m*₀.  
　Proof: (A) Δu = min(cap − load, E) and E ≥ 0, so Δu \< 0 ⇔ cap \< load. When Q\_P·Se ≤ ωt, cap = 0 \< load; otherwise kt(Q\_PSe − ωt) \< σQ\_P/Q ⇔ Q\_P(kt·Se − σ/Q) \< kt·ωt ⇔ Q\_P \< Q*\_e; and Q*\_e \> ωt/Se, so the two cases combine into Q\_P \< Q*\_e. (B) Δc \< 0 ⇔ cap₂ \< load₁ ⇔ S\_c \< (ωt + load₁/kt)/Q\_P (load₁ \> 0; this includes the case Q\_PS\_c ≤ ωt); S\_c = (S0 − (m\_P + ℓ)/Lb)⁺ and the right-hand side is positive, so ⇔ m\_P + ℓ \> m*₀. ∎ (C4c: three lake levels, 14628 cases, no counterexample.) Note: the threshold that includes ℓ, m\* = m*₀ − ℓ, must not be compared with m̄ + ℓ (that would count the lake level twice); always compare the pre-beat m\_P + ℓ with m*₀.  
　Values: for the low flow Q*\_e = 3/2 (a share of exactly 2/3); for the high flow Q*\_e = 32/23 (a share of about 0.348).  
　Theorem 21-10(2′) (Compatibility gate) \[proved\]  Under the declared parameters Q*\_e(S) = θQ\_S. Hence: (1) R*\_θ(P) holds at a low-flow read ⇒ Q\_P ≥ θQ\_S = Q*\_e ⇒ Δu\_P ≥ 0 in that low flow; (2) the low flow buries the mouth of P (0 \< Q\_P \< Q*\_e) ⇒ Q\_P \< θQ\_S ⇒ R\* does not hold at the same read. ∎ (C4d: no counterexample on the grid points or the two histories.) Note: this is a compatibility relation on the same read; 2/3 is the gate given, under these parameters, by the throughflow condition of R; it does not imply “a negative single-beat component this time ⇒ the next read of R fails”.  
　Reading: under the declared parameters, before the law is established the low flow buries the mouth in the flow-divided state, and once the law is established the low-flow read is compatible with not burying the mouth; the single-event answer to “which shallowing makes the route lose stability” is that the gully mouth is buried when the share of its route falls below Q*\_e/Q, and the channel reach backfills when m\_P + ℓ exceeds m*₀.  
5.5  Step Five: Tendency Carried Out as Activity  
5.5.1  Actual Paths and Channel-Exit Sediment Transport of Successive Inflows \[verified\]  
　(Q\_P/Q, S\_P, S\_Q) for each event of H1 = (B·R³)⁸(S·R³)⁸ (C5 output, first three decimal places): high flows 0.54/0.78/0.46 → 0.57/0.82/0.35 → 0.60/0.90/0.24 → 0.64/1.01/0.07 → 0.70/1.16/0 → 0.75/1.30/0 → 0.79/1.39/0 → 0.81/1.46/0; low flows 0.79/0.35/0 → … → 0.80/0.37/0.  
　H2 = (S·R³)⁸(B·R³)⁸: low flows 0.54/0/0 → 0.55/0/0 → 0.54/0/0 → 0.53/0/0 → 0.50/0/0 (constant at 1/2 from the 5th onward); high flows 0.50/0.62/0.62 → … → 0.50/0.47/0.47.  
　Intermittent connectivity (C5a): in the early part of H2 the channel-exit sediment transport of every low flow is 0 (the hillslope sediment is all deposited at the footslope); in the later part of H1 every low flow has channel-exit sediment transport \> 0 through P (every route has the same bar-retention rate β \< 1, so the part entering the lake is also \> 0). \[Conditional conclusion\] The case cited in the central question—“when does a hillslope–valley–lake-basin route that was at first intermittently connected begin to carry the main sediment transport repeatedly in one class of inflow”—has an exact answer in this model: the lake-entering connectivity of the low flow begins in H1 after candidate dominance is generated (from the 9th event on), and never occurs in H2.  
5.5.2  External Signals and Internal Continuation  
　This model has no fast residue r (U1 of D18-7 of \[18\] holds: given the state and the signal, activity is unique); external signals (Q, s\_max, ℓ) and the Trace-Responses left jointly determine activity: the same Q gives different flow division in different states (5.4.1). Under strong lake waves the lakeshore mouth bar is an activity residue (5.1.3), and its effect is carried in the run by the same equations.

### 5.5.3  Budget (C5b, C5c)

　On event beats the budget closes exactly before booking; across beats the separate accounts by process are identical position by position (4.2).  
5.6  Step Six: Activity Acting Again on the Set—Competition Between Two Write-Backs  
5.6.1  D′ᵢ Writes Directly into Tᵢ₊₁ \[verified\]  
　One high flow from frame state w8 (jxs\_core.step of \[18\], original-mechanism semantics): the action set and the affected set are both {V, c\_P, h, m\_P, u\_P, u\_Q}; write-backs u\_P +193/256, c\_P +139/256, m\_P +193/256, u\_Q −11/256, h −1/4, V +193/256 (C6a).  
5.6.2  Competition Between Two Write-Backs  
　Positive write-back: incision of the gully mouth and the channel reach (positive loop), footslope deposition on the opposite route (lowering g\_Q). Negative write-back: the route's own mouth bar (negative loop) and the channel-reach backfilling it causes through backwater (Theorem 21-5(B)), hillslope diffusive infilling.  
　Proposition 21-6 (End-of-macro-step upper bound on the mouth bar) \[proved\]  Assume that each event is followed by at least r rest beats, that at most one mouth-bar perturbation +Δ is applied at the end of each macro-step, and at most b over the whole run. Write ρ = (1 − μ)^r, out\_max = max\_e\[s\_max(e) + ke(Q\_eSe − ωc)⁺ + ke(Q\_eS0 − ωc)⁺\], m̄\_j = β\_j·out\_max·ρ/(1 − ρ), where β\_j is the actual bar-retention rate of route j (baseline β\_P = β\_Q = β = 1/2; under the sediment-destination switch β\_P takes the declared value, and β\_P = 0 gives m̄\_P = 0) or any upper bound of it. Then at the end of the k-th macro-step m\_j(k) ≤ M + b\_k·Δ, M := max(m\_j(0), m̄\_j), where b\_k is the number of perturbations already applied in the first k macro-steps.  
　Proof: out ≤ σ + E + E₂ ≤ out\_max (the component bounds of Lemma 21-0). One macro-step: on the event beat m′ ≤ m + β\_j·out\_max, the r rest beats multiply by no more than ρ (floor and clipping only decrease, never increase), and the perturbation adds at most Δ. Induction: assume m(k) ≤ M + jΔ; then m(k + 1) ≤ ρ(M + jΔ + β\_j·out\_max) + \[perturbation\]Δ ≤ ρ(M + β\_j·out\_max) + jΔ + \[perturbation\]Δ, and ρ(M + β\_j·out\_max) ≤ M (when M ≥ m̄\_j: ρ(m̄\_j + β\_j·out\_max) = m̄\_j, and ρ(M + β\_j·out\_max) grows in M at the rate ρ \< 1). If an upper bound β̄ ≥ β\_j is substituted, every inequality still holds. ∎ Declared baseline value m̄ = 243/296 ≈ 0.821 (C6b: no exceedance in H1 or H2; no exceedance in 16 segment runs with two initial values and 1 perturbation Δ = 1/16). Note: the bound constrains only the ends of macro-steps; right after an event beat the value can reach m(k) + β\_j·out\_max.  
　Corollary 21-6a (Sufficient condition for no backfilling) \[proved\]  If, in the run considered, the pre-beat own-route throughflow of every event is no lower than Q\_P^min and the sediment leaving the gully mouth is no higher than l^max (l^max \> 0), and M + bΔ + ℓ\_max \< m*₀(Q\_P^min, l^max), then Δc\_P ≥ 0 on every event beat. Proof: m*₀ is increasing in Q\_P and decreasing in load₁, so m*₀(Q\_P, load₁) ≥ m*₀(Q\_P^min, l^max); m\_P before an event beat is the value at the end of the previous macro-step and, by Proposition 21-6, does not exceed M + bΔ; the claim follows by Theorem 21-5(B). ∎ Counterexample F21-1 (μ = 3/4: m̄ = 1/56, m\_P(0) = 2, first-beat Q\_P = 48/17, load₁ = 25/34, m*₀ = 17/16, Δc = −25/68) is correctly excluded by this corollary: M = 2 \> m*₀ (C4e). Under the declared baseline M = m̄ ≈ 0.821, whereas the high-flow Q\_P^min = 8/3 and l^max = 5/4 give m\*₀ = 9/16, so the sufficient condition is not met; under the declared baseline the holding of dominance comes not from this corollary but from the exhaustive adjudication of Section 8.  
　No backfilling must be kept apart from the absence of negative feedback (C6c): on w8 this high flow does not backfill the channel reach (Δc \> 0), yet the mouth bar still reduces the throughflow of P through the sill height—removing the mouth bar raises Q\_P from 3.3511 to 3.5724. Corollary 21-6a excludes only one negative write-back, backfilling, and does not exclude the sill-height action of the mouth bar.  
5.6.3  Variants and Prolonged Low Flow: Test Results (Necessity Not Issued) \[verified\]  
　The same H1, changing only one parameter (C6d; listing the reads, out of 16, at which the main relation holds; Dom\_8 is under protocol P8, see 6.3):  
　　Declared baseline: reads 5–16 hold; Dom\_8 00001111111111111.  
　　Strong backwater Lb = 3/4: the main relation never holds; Dom\_8 all 0.  
　　Medium backwater Lb = 1: reads 6–10 hold; Dom\_8 all 0—the main relation holds at 5 consecutive reads, yet Dom\_8 is never 1.  
　　Weak lake waves μ = 1/8: reads 6–14 hold; Dom\_8 00000000111100000.  
　　High flood-season lake level ℓ = 1/2 (in phase with the inflow): reads 4–13 hold; Dom\_8 00011111111100000.  
　These are only results at the parameter points tested and do not issue “that negative write-back does not win is a necessary condition for generation”; to issue necessity, a measurable condition for “negative write-back wins” must first be given and proved (U21-9).  
　Low flows only, over the long term (C6e): from w8, with only low flows arriving, the main relation first fails at read 29 (share 0.6657); at that point the series bottleneck is the channel reach (c\_P = 79/256 \< u\_P = 49/128), and in that event the gully mouth and the channel reach are still both net scouring—the failure comes from the accumulation of rest infilling, not from mouth burial. This is a test result.  
5.7  Step Seven: Dynamics Types and Verification Judgments  
5.7.1  Verdicts on the Same T and the Same Frame \[verified\] (C10)  
　T = {u\_P, c\_P}, frame Φ8.  
　TraceSet (C10a, verified separately): both positions of T have signal-formation evidence (a difference from the reference history exceeding ε), are Trace-Response carriers by the three-way split, and pass K1–K3 (5.2.1); for the organization edges see 5.3.3 (the co-participation hyperedges of {u\_P, c\_P}).  
　HDyn (C10b): on the later part of the formative history (the beats of high flows 5–8), by Definition 6′ of \[17\]: the component 0.5523 of process scour\_e on u\_P at beat 16 is effective; a valid contrast that changes only the retained Trace-Response position u\_P of that beat (set to 0) makes the total-component difference −0.2987 exceed ε; A = −0.2987, D = 0, and the difference is borne by the activity-mediated branch. The bearing positions were formed in high flows 1–4, before that beat (at the end of macro-step 4 the depths of both positions exceed ε). Value: effective difference certified.  
　Part (C10c): on the same T and the same frame state w8, making one position of T shallower changes the flow division of one high flow.  
　Bearing chain (C10e): at beat 16, the starting beat of the later part on which C10b sits, with the positions outside T fixed at their actual values, the organization graph of T = {u\_P, c\_P} alone (exact enumeration per Definition 5′ of \[17\], probes B and S) has 6 edges—the co-participation edges Q\_P, Q\_Q, S\_P, S\_Q and the history-condition edges scour\_c@c\_P, scour\_e@u\_P—forming 1 component {c\_P, u\_P}; a Part witness exists for a high flow at the same beat (read-out Q\_P); the differences of the two positions from the rest reference are u\_P 67/64 and c\_P 109/128 (both \> ε). This chain shows what bears Fld in the local model: at the same beat, the same T is organized within itself (co-participation and history-condition edges), changes the read-out (Part), and changes the write of that beat through the activity-mediated branch (HDyn). The prior history provides two kinds of evidence: formation evidence—the above differences were written by the first 4 high flows (they are absent in the rest reference); retention evidence—under the three-way split, both positions are Trace-Response carriers under rest after supply cut-off from the frame state (C1, C10a). For u\_Q the gridding method is not changed: its lower bound and persistence are issued only within floor-gridding semantics (Theorem 21-3(2), U21-8).  
　Fld (C10d): the three are obtained separately on the same T and the same frame and then conjoined; in model mode Fld = 1 (Column A). All three are evaluated on the same T; conclusions on different T are not conjoined. The R-layer event-witness path is kept (6.2 of \[18\]; the law does not belong to D12 of \[S2\] and does not enter the F layer).  
　Real landforms: no measured verdict is issued; there is no intervention at the level of the Trace-Responses, so the measured verdict of HDyn is ⊥\_Obs (not adjudicated as 0).  
5.7.2  Dynamics Types (registered in the vocabulary of \[S16\]; registration provides no theorem transfer)  
　Φ1a, Φ14, Φ15, Φ2, Φ3a, Φ5a, Φ13; the lake basin is a Φ4b-type side-branch record (not in the element set). Host-specific mechanisms: global coupling under the conservation constraint; the same activity writing both positive and negative write-backs at once; erasure by mouth burial; masking by supply saturation. Interface with \[C-2\] of \[S16\]: N13 corresponds; N12 corresponds only in part, and this model has no self-release of head impounded behind the dam; N15 is not covered.  
5.7.3  Summary of What the Seven Steps Bear  
　Step one: the three-way split under the unified frame Φ8. Step two: the element certificates E and the deepening, shallowing, saturation, addition and elimination of single Trace-Responses (including the gridding bias). Step three: the organization hypergraph on E and the necessary condition for joint formation. Step four: tendency and the positive and negative loops, the single-event threshold and the compatibility gate. Step five: the flow division and channel-exit sediment transport of successive inflows, intermittent connectivity, the budget in separate accounts. Step six: the distribution of write-backs, the mouth-bar upper bound, the sufficient condition for no backfilling, the test variants. Step seven: TraceSet, Part, HDyn and Fld on the same T; the dominance adjudication of the candidate law and the model-side alignment with the qualification of the Law Group Form (Section 8).

6\. Equations and Criteria

### 6.1  One-Beat Equations

　State w ∈ ∏\_x \[lo\_x, hi\_x\] ∩ G⁻¹ℤ (grid G = 256). One-beat signal s ∈ {B, S} (events), R (rest), F (freeze-thaw). Activity y = act(w, s) (Q\_P, Q\_Q, S\_P, S\_Q on event beats; 0 on rest and freeze-thaw beats). Write components comps(w, y, s) = {(process, position, component)}; the one-beat map is  
　　w′ = Grid ∘ Clamp ( w + Σ\_{(p, x, c) ∈ comps(w, y, s)} c·e\_x ),  
　where Clamp truncates each position to \[lo\_x, hi\_x\] and Grid applies floor(x·G)/G position by position (executed by jhc\_core.Host.step of Appendix A.1 of \[17\]). Macro-step = one event beat followed by r = 3 rest beats (and at most one declared perturbation). This is the discrete form of D′ᵢ writing directly into Tᵢ₊₁ (5.6.1).

### 6.2  Relations and Readings

　Main relation R\*\_θ(j) (θ = 2/3): when event e arrives, Q\_j ≥ θQ\_e, and S\_j \> 0, S\_j ≥ θ(S\_P + S\_Q) (4.3). Readings take only the actual flow division and the actual channel-exit sediment amounts of event beats; the same set of readings is used for all comparisons, and the readings do not change between before and after formation.  
　Relation types (Table 21-1): Table 21-1 lists, for each relation and predicate, the argument types, the quantifiers and whether a closure may be taken; the type annotations change no equation and no reading.  
　　(1) R*\_θ: a ternary predicate whose arguments are (the state w before the event beat, event e ∈ {B, S}, route j ∈ {P, Q}), R*\_θ(w, e, j) ⇔ Q\_j(w, e) ≥ θQ\_e ∧ S\_j(w, e) \> 0 ∧ S\_j(w, e) ≥ θ(S\_P + S\_Q)(w, e). It is not a binary comparison relation on one set, and reflexivity, symmetry, antisymmetry and transitivity do not apply. Composite readings along a history each state their quantifiers: Dom\_N is universal over future sequences and over reads (next item), and the re-traversal record of Ω\_W is an in-window count ≥ 2 (8.5(3)).  
　　(2) Dom\_N: a predicate with arguments (state w, frozen protocol 𝓟), taking the value 1 or 0 (6.3). Dom\_N(w; 𝓟) = 1 ⇔ for every event–perturbation sequence satisfying 𝓟, and for each of the first N + 1 reads on it, R\*\_θ(·, e, P) holds for the upcoming event of that read; Dom\_N = 0 ⇔ there exist an admissible sequence and a read on it at which it fails. Dom\_∞ is the greatest fixed point on the product state set (6.3(C)). It too is not a comparison relation, and the four properties above do not apply.  
　　(3) The subitems of the structural positive end (8.5(3)): predicates with arguments (carrying organization R, end of macro-step t). CurrentCarrier is universal over the positions of R; CarrierRealizes and ΩSupport are universal over i ∈ I; DynamicRole is existential (there exist i ∈ I and a legitimate counterfactual v). The structural positive end is the conjunction Established ∧ DynamicRole ∧ ΩSupport on the same (R, t); for the subitems of Established see 8.5(3).  
　　(4) Co-participation hyperedges (5.3.3): the endpoints are (an activity component Q\_j or S\_j, a subset U of the element certificate set E), meaning that the positions in U jointly determine that activity component; the positions in U have equal standing, and the edge is undirected; no transitive closure is taken—two co-participation hyperedges sharing one position does not imply that the remaining positions of the two edges jointly determine the same activity component.  
　　(5) History-condition hyperedges (5.3.3): the endpoints are (a setting subset U ⊆ E, the written process and position p@x), meaning that the setting of U changes the write component of process p on position x; directed, from U to p@x (the node set of the hyperedge is written U ∪ {x}); no transitive closure is taken—U → p@x together with U′ ∋ x and U′ → p′@x′ does not imply U → p′@x′, and transitivity over several beats is accounted for separately by multi-beat write-back.  
　　(6) Connectivity of the organization graph: reading both kinds of hyperedge, (4) and (5), as undirected membership relations among the positions they contain, the reflexive, symmetric and transitive closure of “two positions lie in the same hyperedge” is an equivalence relation on E, whose equivalence classes are the components reported in 5.3.3 (C3e: one component). This connectivity equivalence is a derived relation, kept apart from direct edges: two positions in the same component need not share a direct edge, and components give neither direction nor write order.  
　　(7) Indistinguishability of candidate histories (10.2): fix a history family 𝓗 and an observation map Y (terrain, hillslope, lake-basin cumulative total, annual layers, event layers, or a combination of them); h \~\_Y h′ ⇔ Y(h) = Y(h′). It is defined by equality of the values of a fixed map, so on a fixed 𝓗 it is reflexive, symmetric and transitive, an equivalence relation; the exact class counts reported in 10.2 are the numbers of its equivalence classes. If Y′ = φ ∘ Y, then \~\_Y ⊆ \~\_{Y′}, and classes only merge (10.2(C)). Approximate indistinguishability at finite resolution, “each component differs by no more than 2e”, is not transitive and is not an equivalence relation, so 10.2(D) adjudicates it pair by pair and does not classify by it.

### 6.3  Candidate Dominance Predicate

　Definition 21-4 (In-model dominance predicate of the candidate law) \[declared: frozen before adjudication\]  The candidate law L\* is given by the relation R\*\_θ(P) (4.3, 6.2: the route carries the main throughflow and the main channel-exit sediment transport) and the domain of validity I = {B, S}. Frozen protocol 𝓟 = (𝓒, r, 𝒟, b, N):  
　　𝓒: inflow class. 𝓒\_K: events taken from {B, S}, with no more than K − 1 consecutive low flows.  
　　r: the number of rest beats after each event.  
　　𝒟: perturbation class, each perturbation applied at the end of some macro-step (after the rest beats) and followed by clipping and gridding.  
　　b: upper limit on the number of perturbations within the adjudication horizon.  
　　N: horizon (number of events).  
　Order within a macro-step: read → event beat → r rest beats → at most one perturbation.  
　(A) Dom\_N(w; 𝓟) = 1 if and only if: in every event–perturbation sequence from w satisfying 𝓟, at every read before the arrival of the 1st through the (N + 1)-th event (N + 1 reads in all; no event is applied after the (N + 1)-th read), R\* holds for the upcoming event of that read. Dom\_N = 0 if and only if there exists an admissible sequence that makes some read fail (i.e., a counterexample).  
　(B) Each adjudication starts afresh from the state w being adjudicated: the consecutive-low-flow count is taken as 0 and the perturbation budget as b, without inheriting the count or the perturbations already used in the actual history. Reason: Dom is a predicate of a state over a frozen class of futures; the actual history itself need not belong to 𝓒 (for example, the 8 consecutive low flows in the later part of H1 do not belong to 𝓒\_2).  
　(C) Dom\_∞(w; 𝓟): N = ∞. The recursion is defined on product states (state w × consecutive-low-flow count c × remaining budget b′): Dom\_∞ is the largest set of product states satisfying “for every admissible event e, R\* holds on w for e, and the product-state successor after the macro-step is still in the set”, and adjudication starts from (w, 0, b).  
　(D) Time of holding: along the end-of-macro-step states X₀, X₁, … of the actual history, t\* := min{k : Dom(X\_k; 𝓟) = 1}. The time of holding says only that dominance first holds at that time; whether it is maintained thereafter is adjudicated separately by the actual readings at the ends of macro-steps along the actual history and by the failure criterion of (F); the formative source (which part of the history is responsible) is adjudicated separately by the truncation–challenge and shift protocols of 8.4; the two are not conflated.  
　(E) Non-exogeneity: if another history from the same X₀ with the same cumulative forcing (the same ΣQ, Σs\_max, actual stripped amount and total number of rest beats) keeps Dom at 0 throughout, then dominance is not given by the inflow class and the cumulative forcing.  
　(F) Failure: some k′ after t\* has Dom(X\_{k′}) = 0.  
　Status: Dom\_N is the in-model dominance-adjudication interface of the candidate law, not the Law qualification of the Law Group Form; for its relation to Law qualification see 8.5. “Law” is not defined as an invariant property of tendency along its own dynamics on a frozen signal class.  
　This paper uses two protocols, with conclusions reported separately:  
　　P8 = (𝓒\_2, r = 3, 𝒟₄ = {id, bar: m\_P += 1/16, bank: c\_P −= 1/16, fill: u\_P −= 1/16}, b = 1, N = 8);  
　　P∞ = (𝓒\_1: high flows only, r = 3, {id, bar′: m\_P += 1/32}, applicable at the end of every macro-step, any number of times, N = ∞).

7\. Running and Solving  
7.1  Exactness, Determinism and Solvability  
　All numerical values are fractions.Fraction; there is no randomness; the programs read only the files of Appendix A (among which the SHA-256 values of the frozen upstream sources A.7 and A.8 are checked by C0a1 and C0a2). Two isolated runs are byte-for-byte identical (Appendix B). The antecedent of Theorem 18-10 of \[18\] (the declared operators can be evaluated exactly in finitely many steps on rational inputs) holds on 𝔐21: L1–L8 are rational expressions and finite piecewise expressions, and post-processing is position-wise clipping and gridding.  
7.2  Monotonicity Lemma (Basis of the Interval Certificates) \[proved\]  
　Lemma 21-0  Under kt·Se ≥ s\_max/Q (for every event; checked by C0b) and h0 + lo \> 0:  
　(1) load₁ = min(cap, load + E) is nondecreasing in Q\_j and σ. Proof: load, cap and E are each nondecreasing in Q\_j, load is increasing in σ, and min preserves nondecrease.  
　(2) out = F(load₁, cap₂, E₂), where F(l, c, e) = l + min(e, (c − l)⁺) − λ(l − c)⁺ is nondecreasing in all three arguments. Proof: for l ≤ c, F = min(l + e, c); for l \> c, F = (1 − λ)l + λc (0 ≤ λ ≤ 1); both expressions are nondecreasing, and they agree at l = c. ∎ Hence out is nondecreasing in Q\_j and σ and nonincreasing in m\_j and ℓ (cap₂ and E₂ are nonincreasing in m through S\_c).  
　(3) Δc = G(load₁, cap₂, E₂), where G(l, c, e) = min(e, (c − l)⁺) − λ(l − c)⁺ is nonincreasing in l and nondecreasing in c and e.  
　(4) Q\_P is nondecreasing in u\_P, c\_P, m\_Q and nonincreasing in u\_Q, c\_Q, m\_P (Lemma 21-1).  
　(5) Clipping and floor gridding are nondecreasing position by position.  
　Hence box\_terms gives exact upper and lower bounds of out from the corner values and a sound envelope of Δc from the monotone corners, Δu uses natural interval extension, and box\_route writes the share as a monotone expression in which each variable appears only once; the interval image of a box is a sound envelope of the true image (the basis of Theorem 21-4(3)).

### 7.3  Decidability

　Theorem 21-4 \[proved\]  (1) Dom\_N is decidable on the declared model: 𝓒\_K, 𝒟 and b are finite, the macro-step map is computable exactly in finitely many steps on rational inputs (7.1), and there are finitely many admissible sequences of length N; deduplicating by (state, count of consecutive low flows, remaining budget) does not change the conclusion (deduplication merges only branches with the same product state, and these have the same future admissible sets). (2) Dom\_∞ is decidable in principle on 𝔐21: the DYN positions are bounded and lie on the grid, the product state space is finite, Dom\_∞ is the greatest fixed point on it, and the set reachable from (w, 0, b) is finite. (3) Soundness of the invariant-box certificate: if a box B ∋ w is such that the interval image of every block in the frozen block class is contained in B, and the interval lower-bound check of R\* holds on the intermediate box before each event within the block, then Dom\_∞(w) = 1. Coverage by blocks: the blocks of P∞ are (B, id) and (B, bar′); every admissible infinite sequence is a concatenation of these two kinds of block, and every finite prefix ends at a block boundary, so the coverage is complete; for the blocks S^jB (j \< K) of 𝓒\_K (K \> 1), prefixes ending in the middle of a block are covered by the event-by-event check on the intermediate boxes within the block. Budget: the box certificate allows a perturbation in every block, so it covers a superset of the budget-limited class, and a certificate on the superset implies the conclusion on the limited class. (4) A finite-depth counterexample is a proof of Dom = 0. ∎ \[Insufficient\] The reachable set in (2) is too large on 𝓒\_2 and above and on 𝒟₄, so Dom\_∞ was not exhaustively enumerated; for 𝓒\_2 only N = 8 is given.

8\. Generation of the Law: Candidate Dominance, Conditions of Generation and Failure, Order Dependence, Historical Localization and Law-Formation Read-Back  
8.1  Definitions of the Four Readings and Their Relations  
　The candidate law L\* is given by the relation R\*\_θ(P) (4.3) and the domain of validity I = {B, S}; it is a candidate given in advance.  
　(1) Candidate dominance Dom\_N (6.3): the in-model dominance predicate of a state over a frozen class of futures; it is not the Law qualification of the Law Group Form.  
　(2) Structural positive end: Established ∧ DynamicRole ∧ ΩSupport holds at t on one and the same carrying organization R (8.5(3)).  
　(3) Current capacity: the finite-horizon capacity under perturbation, Cap\_8 = Dom\_8(P8), and the two-entry cycle without external maintenance (8.5(4)); both are model evidence of the current holding-tendency.  
　(4) The forward read-back of Law: via Theorem 4 of *The Necessity Form of Law*, the structural positive end is read as Law under the three host correspondences and the holding-tendency semantic carry-over (Theorem 21-17).  
　Known relations among the four: in H1, Dom\_8 and Established both first hold at the end of macro-step 4, and the structural positive end first holds at the end of macro-step 10; Cap\_8 holds before the structural positive end does (L5b), so capacity is not read as qualification; without the semantic carry-over the structural positive end is not read as Law. The conditions of generation and failure (8.2), order dependence (8.3), and the time of holding and historical localization (8.4) are all stated in terms of candidate dominance; the law-formation read-back (8.5) is stated in terms of the structural positive end and current capacity.  
8.2  Conditions of Generation and Failure  
　Theorem 21-10 (Summary of the Conditions of Generation)  
　(1) Necessary \[proved\]: Theorem 21-2 (joint incision of the gully mouth and the channel reach; an upper bound on the mouth-bar difference, tightest for the smallest inflow).  
　(2′) Compatibility threshold \[proved\]: 5.4.3.  
　(3′) Test results \[verified\]: the variants of 5.6.3 (necessity not issued).  
　(4) Sufficient \[verified\]: under P8, Dom\_8 = 1 from the end of macro-step 4 of H1 on; under P∞, Dom\_∞ = 1 at the ends of macro-steps 6 and 8 (invariant box).  
　(5) Key Trace-Responses and connection (9.1): the gully-mouth incision and the channel-reach incision of P; the water-passing connection from footslope to gully mouth is tested by true disconnection.  
　Theorem 21-8 (Failure)  
　(A) \[proved\] Static failure: if Theorem 21-2(1) or (2) fails for some Q in the inflow class, then R\* already fails at the next read for that inflow, and Dom = 0.  
　(B) \[verified\] Prolonged low flow only: 5.6.3 (failure at the 29th read; a test result).  
　(C) \[verified\] Variants: 5.6.3 (test results).  
　(D) \[verified\] Interventions: 9.1.

### 8.3  The Order-Dependence Theorem

　Theorem 21-7 (Order Dependence)  X₀ is the declared initial terrain, H1 = (B·R³)⁸(S·R³)⁸, H2 = (S·R³)⁸(B·R³)⁸; the two have the same cumulative inflow 50, cumulative strippable upper limit 5, actual stripping 301/64 and number of rest beats.  
　(A) \[verified: exhaustive decision\] Under P8, Dom\_8 of H1 first equals 1 at the end of macro-step 4 and remains 1 to the end; Dom\_8 of H2 is 0 at all 17 macro-step ends (C7a, C7b).  
　(B) \[proved\] Mouth-burial erasure and symmetry preservation:  
　　(1) Event beat: if in a low flow the shares of both routes satisfy Q\_j·max(Se, S0) ≤ min(ωt, ωc), then at that event beat Δu\_j = −σQ\_j/Q, Δc\_j = 0, out\_j = 0, and m\_j and V do not change at that event beat. Proof: cap = E = 0, Δu = min(−load, 0) = −load, load₁ = 0; for the channel reach cap₂ = E₂ = 0 and d = 0, so Δc = 0 and out = 0. ∎ This clause is restricted to event beats: rest beats still rework the mouth bar by L8 (Counterexample F21-2: m\_P = 1/4, the low-flow threshold is satisfied, m\_P is still 1/4 after the event beat and 27/256 after three rest beats, C7d).  
　　(2) Symmetry preservation: suppose that at some beat the state satisfies u\_P = u\_Q, c\_P = c\_Q, m\_P = m\_Q (the state at which the two routes first coincide is symmetric position by position); that all subsequent signals are symmetric with respect to the two routes (the laws of event, rest and freeze-thaw beats have the same form for P and Q); that post-processing (clipping and gridding) is position-wise and commutes with the swap π: P ↔ Q; and that no perturbation acting on only one route is applied. Then at every subsequent beat the two routes are identical position by position, Q\_P = Q/2, and R*\_θ(P) and R*\_θ(Q) (θ \> 1/2) fail at every read. Proof: π preserves L1–L8 and the post-processing, so it is a constitutive automorphism in the sense of \[18\] D18-5; by \[18\] Theorem 18-2(1) (equivariance under original-mechanism semantics), the successor of a symmetric state is again symmetric; a symmetric state has g\_P = g\_Q and m\_P = m\_Q, so Q\_P = Q/2 by L2. ∎  
　　(3) The instance on H2 (C7e): every low flow in the first part of H2 satisfies the antecedent of (1); c and m of both routes are 0 from the start, (1) guarantees that event beats do not change them, and rest beats keep 0 at 0; the two mouths reach the burial limit together at beat 13, at which point the state is symmetric position by position; from then to the last beat the two routes are identical position by position and the share stays at 1/2. H2 starts from m = 0.  
　(C) \[verified\] After erasure the winner is decided by a perturbation (C7g): on the symmetric state at the end of macro-step 8 of H2, add 1/128 to u\_P or to u\_Q, then apply 16 high flows; in each case the route that received the addition takes the immediate main relation in the final state.  
　(D) The final organizations differ, and so do the cumulative lake-basin amounts (1793/256 vs. 289/64, C9g).  
　Reading: of two histories with the same cumulative forcing in different orders, under P8 one acquires dominance of the candidate law and the other does not; by 8.4(C)(D), the segments that bear the difference are “the first 4 high flows” and “the first 4 low flows”, that is, the temporal relation “the high flows amplify the old Trace-Response above the burial threshold before the low flows bury the mouth”.  
8.4  Time of Holding, Formative Source and Historical Localization \[verified\]  
　(A) P8 (C7a): Dom\_8 of H1 first equals 1 at the end of macro-step 4 (after 4 high flows); the counterexample at the end of macro-step 3 is “the read at the next high flow already fails”. Replacing 𝓒\_2 by 𝓒\_4 leaves the first time unchanged.  
　(B) P∞ (C7j): there are invariant-box certificates at the ends of macro-steps 6 and 8; at the end of macro-step 4 no certificate is obtained (⊥, owing to interval wrapping; not adjudicated as 0); the initial terrain has a counterexample. The invariant box at the end of macro-step 8: u\_P ∈ \[555/256, 79/32\], c\_P ∈ \[419/256, 79/32\], m\_P ∈ \[7/16, 87/128\], u\_Q ∈ \[−15/64, −15/256\], c\_Q ∈ \[0, 13/256\], m\_Q = 0, h ∈ \[3/64, 19/8\]. P8 and P∞ are different protocols; the conclusion at macro-step 4 and the infinite-horizon certificates are not read together.  
　(C) Truncation–challenge protocol (C7h): the first k high flows are followed by the same challenge (S·R³)⁸; R\* holds at every read during the challenge and Dom\_8 = 1 in the final state if and only if k ≥ 4. \[Conditional conclusion\] Under the same subsequent challenge, the formative part that bears candidate dominance is the first 4 high flows.  
　(D) Shift protocol (C7i): for S^jB⁸S^(8−j), with the same cumulative totals, Dom\_8 still holds for j ≤ 3 (the first time moves later in turn: the ends of macro-steps 4, 5, 6, 8), and for j ≥ 4 it is 0 at all 17 macro-step ends. \[Conditional conclusion\] The 4th preceding low flow completes the mouth-burial erasure (consistent with the two mouths reaching the burial limit together at beat 13 of H2).  
　(E) δ dependence (C7g′, recomputed with the same Dom\_8(P8)): on the symmetric state at the end of macro-step 8 of H2, add δ to u\_P and then apply 16 high flows; the macro-step at which Dom\_8 first equals 1 is 13, 7, 3, 1 respectively (δ = 1/128, 1/32, 1/8, 1/4), the same as the macro-step at which the immediate main relation first holds for both inflow classes simultaneously, and once established it is not lost again within 16 macro-steps.  
　(F) Stripping and grid for the two histories (C0c, C7f): the actual hillslope stripping of H1 and of H2 is 301/64, listed separately from the cumulative strippable upper limit 5. For G = 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, the immediate R\* in the final state of H1 holds for both inflow classes, the immediate R\* in the final state of H2 holds for neither inflow, and the two gully mouths are equally deep; for burial limits −1/4, −1/2, −3/4, −7/8, the final state of H2 is symmetric in every case. Only these final-state readings are issued; invariance of t\* or Dom\_8 under the grid is not issued.  
8.5  Law-Formation Read-Back: Candidate Relation, Frozen Declaration, Host Correspondences and Conditional Theorem (dm21\_law.py, dm21\_run\_law.py)  
(1) Choice of the Candidate Relation and Its Rationale  
　Only one relation is chosen: the main-route relation L\* of route P in the local model 𝔐21, Rel(L*) = R*\_θ(P) (θ = 2/3). Reasons: (1) it does not hold on the initial terrain (L0a: the P share is 0.5495 for both inflow classes), so it satisfies “did not hold at first”; (2) the topology of 𝔐21 is fixed, the Gully-Line identity required by Ω\_W is unique, and maintenance of the connection can be checked directly by actual conductance and actual throughflow; (3) Sections 8 and 9 already give order comparisons, interventions and Dom certificates for the same relation, so the states before and after formation can be compared under the same criterion. The N succession relation in the extended model is not chosen: its Ω\_W would require separate verification of disconnection between events, same-path identity and succession (variable paths), and no perturbation challenge is frozen on 𝔐21⁺, so no current-capacity quantity is available for the second way of supplying SC\_Hold; the read-back there is not closed and is listed as a gap (14.2).  
(2) Frozen Declaration \[declared\] (written into dm21\_law.py and the L0 output of dm21\_run\_law.py before running)  
　L*: when event i arrives, route P carries the main throughflow (Q\_P ≥ θQ\_i) and the main channel-exit sediment transport (S\_P \> 0 and S\_P ≥ θ(S\_P + S\_Q)). I = I\_cov = {B, S} (L* claims applicability to both inflow classes; coverage polarity). R = {u\_P, c\_P}; the Trace-Response positions outside R are {m\_P, u\_Q, c\_Q, m\_Q} (h is the sediment-supply reservoir and V is never read; neither is a candidate for carrying the relation). W = 8 macro-steps ending at t, divided into 2 equal segments; the re-traversal record criterion is ≥ 2. The legitimate counterfactual family for DynamicRole: reverting the increments relative to the rest reference (keeping the preset base; both positions, u\_P alone, c\_P alone) and resetting to the ground state, with the other positions fixed. The current-capacity quantity is Cap\_8 = Dom\_8(P8) (input condition 𝓒\_2; maintenance conditions 𝒟₄, budget 1; evaluation horizon N = 8; criterion: all N + 1 reads of R\* hold). History family 𝓗\_10: all {B, S} event sequences of length ≤ 10 from the initial terrain.  
(3) The Three Host Correspondences (Corr; Declared Reading in Model Mode, Column A)  
　The read-back does not pass through the formal layer of *The Mathematical Form of Law* (it does not invoke the H\_Read group); it reads directly in 𝔐21 by the definitions of the structural predicates in *The General Form of Law*. This “direct reading” is itself a declaration, and it is written out item by item. The sufficient direction of *The Necessity Form of Law* does not pass through G2 read-back and does not carry C0, C2, H1–H3 (*The Master Map of the Law Group Form* 3.3, 6.2), so the read-back here does not take the conditions of the necessary direction as antecedents.  
　Corr-E (Established, *The General Form of Law* Section 2): CurrentCarrier ↔ both positions of R have oriented depth \> ε, and both belong to the series route of P (an organizational block connected by “continuation between Structural Positions” in the sense of Definition 3.34 of *The Mathematical Form of Law*); CarrierRealizes (coverage) ↔ for every i ∈ I, Read\_i holds; with R reset to the ground state, Read\_i fails (the content of the relation is carried by R); with the Trace-Response positions outside R reset to the ground state and R kept, Read\_i still holds (R is the complete carrying; no superstructure is inferred from a qualifying substructure); CarrierNonInstant (coverage) ↔ Lemma 21-13; sedimentary carrying ↔ under supply cut-off and rest from t, the \[17\] three-way split classifies every position of R as Trace-Response carrier. In all counterfactuals and resets, h, as the sediment-supply boundary, keeps its actual value (the Trace-Response positions outside R are only m\_P, u\_Q, c\_Q, m\_Q); this correspondence is certified on that working domain. The content of the H1 bridge (CC ∧ CR ∧ CNI ⇒ Established) is checked here directly by the item “sedimentary carrying”, without invoking H1.  
　Lemma 21-13 (Non-instantaneity at macro-step ends) \[proved\]  All state positions of 𝔐21 are structural or sedimentary positions; inflow enters only at event beats, via act; there is no inflow at macro-step ends, Drive^inst = ∅, the freeze Freeze\_t(F) is the identity, and Estab\_t = Existing\_t (*The Mathematical Form of Law* Definitions 3.2–3.4). Hence, when reading at a macro-step end, the complete carrying by R for each i does not depend on any link maintained only by the present instantaneous input: CarrierNonInstant ⇔ CarrierRealizes. ∎  
　Corr-D (DynamicRole, *The General Form of Law* Section 3, existential polarity): there exist i ∈ I and a legitimate counterfactual v (other positions fixed) such that both the actual activity of the duty on event i (Q\_P, S\_P) and its write-back (Δu\_P, Δc\_P) change. The target is the actual activity and write-back of the duty; the witnesses of DynamicRole^h (beat 33, and the rest-diffusion component at beat 1 of H1m, 8.6) are issued only for the processes they prove and are not used for this item.
