　Schumm 1973 (*Geomorphic thresholds and complex response of drainage systems*, Chapter 13; main-text level; the text read does not give the book title, editors or page numbers): a geomorphic threshold is “a threshold developed within the system by changes of the system itself through time”; the distinction between extrinsic and intrinsic thresholds; complex response: in a small-basin experiment with a 10 cm base-level lowering, the main channel first incised upstream from its mouth and formed a terrace, sediment from upstream made the newly incised channel aggrade, and after the tributaries adjusted, sediment supply decreased and the channel incised again, forming a lower terrace. \| Adopted: complex response is this paper's “one activity writing positive and negative write-backs at once”; intrinsic thresholds are this paper's Q*\_e and m*. \| Added: intrinsic thresholds are written as exact single-event criteria, and complex response is advanced to adjudications of the generation and failure of laws.  
　Phillips 2006 (Hydrology and Earth System Sciences 10: 731–742; abstract level) and Phillips 2003 (Progress in Physical Geography; bibliographic-record level): nonlinear geomorphic systems can exhibit dynamical instability and chaos, with multiple possible response trajectories or modes of adjustment; geomorphic systems are path dependent and historically and geographically contingent. \| Adopted: path dependence as a background proposition. \| Added: in a mechanistic model, this paper gives the bearers of path dependence (which Trace-Response, which connection) and an adjudicable concept of “law”, and gives when history leaves no trace (Theorem 21-9(A)): path dependence is not universal but conditional.  
　Jerolmack and Paola 2010 (GRL 37: L19401; main-text level): sediment transport can act as a nonlinear filter, and signals with periods shorter than a characteristic time Tx are erased unless they are large enough; for T/Tx no greater than about 0.6 there is no evidence of periodicity in the output flux; signals whose amplitude exceeds the largest clearing events can pass through. \| Adopted: “signal shredding” as the competing background for the climate-trace layer. \| Added: this paper gives two position-by-position exact “no-trace” mechanisms, exchange indistinguishability (the freeze-thaw beat map commutes with the other beat maps, Theorem 21-9(A)) and burial erasure (Theorem 21-7(B)), and gives when joint readings can recover order.  
　Romans, Castelltort, Covault, Fildani and Walsh (Earth-Science Reviews 153: 7–29, year listed as 2015 on the page read; abstract level): assesses the propagation of environmental signals through sediment routing systems, with sediment flux as the carrier of upstream forcing; autogenic dynamics and temporary storage and release can introduce noise or lag into external forcing signals, or mask them completely. \| Adopted: the hillslope regolith as temporary storage (h in this paper), whose “masking” occurs exactly under saturated sediment supply (Theorem 21-9(A)).  
12.7  Climate and Geomorphology: Freeze-Thaw, Precipitation Variability and Lake Sediment Records  
　Hales and Roering 2007 (JGR 112, DOI 10.1029/2006JF000616; bibliographic-record level, abstract not obtained): the title concerns climatic controls on frost cracking and their implications for the evolution of bedrock landscapes. \| Adopted: only as the bibliographic source for the candidate mechanism “freeze-thaw produces regolith”; the freeze-thaw production rate ft in L8 of this paper is a constructed value, and no value or relation from that paper is cited.  
　Tucker and Bras 2000 (Water Resources Research 36: 1953–1964; bibliographic-record level): the title concerns a stochastic approach to the role of rainfall variability in drainage basin evolution. \| Adopted: only as the bibliographic source for “precipitation intensity and timing affect erosion”; its conclusions are not cited as evidence.  
　Zolitschka, Francus, Ojala and Schimmelmann 2015 (Quaternary Science Reviews 117: 1–41; abstract level): lake varves form because of the seasonality of sedimentary processes and provide direct annual dating; varves can be clastic, biogenic or endogenic, depending on the dominant climatic and depositional conditions. \| Adopted: lake-basin event layers and annual layers as readings of climate traces (Section 10). \| Added: in a mechanistic model, a quantitative comparison of how well the resolutions of three readings, “event layers, annual layers, cumulative amounts”, distinguish climatic timing (Theorem 21-9(C)).  
12.8  Specific Null Hypotheses of Competing Theories (for the points of distinction in Section 13)  
　E (a specific null hypothesis of steady-state equilibrium): under fixed subsequent driving, the system has a unique attracting state, and that state is independent of prior history. What is taken is its long-run consequence: for two histories with the same cumulative totals but different orders, under a common fixed subsequent driving, the final states eventually converge. A unique attracting state does not imply monotone or measurable convergence within a finite window. Counterexample F21-7 (attracting but not monotone): state set {0, 1, 2}, f(0) = 0, f(1) = 2, f(2) = 0; 0 is the unique attracting state (every initial state reaches 0 within at most two steps), yet for the two orbits starting from 0 and 1 the stepwise distances \|x − y\| are 1, 2, 0, first growing and then converging. Hence a growing difference, or non-convergence, within a finite window does not breach E; non-convergence within any finite window fixed in advance cannot by itself breach the E declared here. To test with a finite window, a separate, independently breachable contraction null hypothesis E\_c must be set up: declare in advance a distance, a resolution threshold and a window length, and predict that the difference within the window is monotone non-increasing and below the resolution threshold at the end of the window; a breach of E\_c narrows only E\_c and does not bear on E. E\_c is not declared.  
　Finite-window readings in the declared model are listed separately: the final states of H1 and H2 do not converge within the 30 events of the common subsequent driving (B·R³·S·R³·S·R³)¹⁰ (from H1 the main relation holds at every reading; from H2 the two routes are position-by-position symmetric throughout; C9f). This reading neither confirms nor excludes E; whether this model converges over an infinite horizon is not adjudicated.  
　O′ (a variant with a unique globally optimal network): if the selection principle gives a unique globally optimal configuration determined only by the forcing, then order does not matter. Note that O′ is not the claim of the work of Rinaldo et al.: that work states explicitly that accessible local optima are diverse and carry the imprint of initial conditions (12.3); the null hypothesis takes only this specific variant, O′.  
　M (rule-based temporal memory): a random walk with the memory rule “erase after n steps without a visit” (as used by Reitz et al. 2010). It allows erasure by time but does not allow filling to change memory; this paper predicts that filling (with no time needed) suffices to erase (P21-2). Healing-type memory rules (Martin and Edmonds 2022) allow filling to change memory and fall outside the scope of M.

　Schumm 1973, Chapter 13, *Geomorphic thresholds and complex response of drainage systems* \| main text (Oregon State University seminar PDF; book title, editors and page numbers not given in the text read)

13\. Breachable Predictions and Notes on Observation  
13.1  Notes on Observation (applying to all predictions in this section)  
　(1) Reading map: u\_j, c\_j ↔ the flow cross-section depths (relative to the initial surface) of the mouth reach at the footslope and of the downstream channel reach; m\_j ↔ the crest height of the sand bar at the lake mouth; share ↔ the flow ratio of the two routes (dye or a flow-dividing weir); S\_j ↔ channel-exit sediment flux (at the channel outlet cross-section); where the channels differ in mouth sand-retention rate, the actual lake-entering deposition flux is also measured and read separately; ℓ ↔ lake-surface elevation.  
　(2) Reading points: the model reads first and then steps. At the end of each macro-step the capacities for the two inflow types are read once (with normal conductance), and only then are the event beat and the three rest beats after it run, with the next reading at the end of the next macro-step. Hence an “end-of-macro-step reading” is a capacity reading at that time point, not a measured flow during some event beat in between; the measured flow of a disconnected event itself is zero and must not be conflated with the end-of-macro-step reading.  
　(3) Observation window: when replicating the protocol of this model, observe at least eight complete macro-steps after a backfill or disconnection treatment, and explicitly schedule recovery readings for both inflow types at the end of the eighth macro-step; recording flow only up to the arrival of the eighth inflow misses one end-of-macro-step reading point. Replication must keep the same driving and intervention conditions; if the probing itself rewrites structure (e.g., tracers or flow measurement alter the bed), use separate arms or independent repeat measurements. In natural field settings the observation window must be declared in advance according to the local inflow regime and sampling design; this model gives no universal lower bound of “eight inflows”.  
　(4) Reading a breach: a breach narrows only the corresponding derivation and its antecedent group and does not breach theorems within the model; “an effect not observed on some occasion” is not read as “no historical dynamics”; when a predicted recovery fails to appear in the field, one can only go on to test whether the treatment changed the structure, and cannot pin down the cause from the failure of recovery alone.  
13.2  Predictions of the Local Model and of Law-Formation Read-Back (P21-1–P21-7, P21-11–P21-13)  
Format: antecedents (declared conditions) → reading map (which measured quantity each model quantity corresponds to) → prediction → breach criterion → point of distinction from competing theories. All predictions are \[Prediction\], Column B, and untested; a breach narrows only the corresponding derivation and its antecedent group and does not breach theorems within the model; “an effect not observed on some occasion” is not read as “no historical dynamics”.  
P21-1  Order dependence (preferred experiment)  
　Antecedents: the same initial topography (a shallow old gully mouth and a symmetric alternative flow path pre-cut, or two nearly symmetric routes on a natural fan surface); the two groups of runs have the same total inflow and sediment supply and the same numbers of high and low flows, and only the order is exchanged (high-flow segment first versus low-flow segment first); the sediment supply of the low-flow segment overloads the footslope under divided flow (sand drops at the footslope), and in the high-flow segment both routes are above threshold; constant lake level; after each segment the flow is stopped and the surface scanned.  
　Reading map: u\_j, c\_j ↔ the flow cross-section depths (relative to the initial surface) of the mouth reach at the footslope and of the downstream channel reach; m\_j ↔ the crest height of the sand bar at the lake mouth; share ↔ the flow ratio of the two routes (dye or a flow-dividing weir); S\_j ↔ channel-exit sediment flux (at the channel outlet cross-section); when the actual lake-entering amount of every route equals its channel-exit amount times one common positive proportionality coefficient, its share equals the share of actual lake-entering deposition flux; otherwise measure and read separately; when lake-entering sediment transport is estimated route by route from differences in lake-basin deposit volume, the contributions of the individual routes must be distinguishable, and the mass budget of outlets, reworking and the observation window must be declared.  
　Prediction: the high-flow-first group forms a single main pathway, and later low flows also reach the lake through it; in the low-flow-first group both mouths are buried by footslope deposits, after which high flows scour out two nearly equal pathways with a flow ratio close to 1, and low flows do not reach the lake.  
　Breach criterion: across several independent runs that satisfy the antecedents (the low-flow segment does overload the footslope under divided flow, and both routes are indeed above threshold in the high-flow segment), the distributions of final flow ratios of the two groups do not differ, or the low-flow-first group also forms a single main pathway that coincides with the pre-cut old mouth.  
　Finite-window readings and long-run comparison listed separately (12.8 E): (1) Finite window: compare the final states of the two groups within the finite window the experiment allows, against the finite-window prediction of this model (they differ; under the same subsequent driving, this model also predicts non-convergence within 30 events, C9f); this tests only the finite-window prediction of this model, not E. (2) Long run: E predicts only eventual convergence under common subsequent driving; before comparison, a distance (e.g., an upper bound on the pointwise differences of the two groups' scanned topographies, or the difference of flow ratios), a resolution threshold and an observation window length must be declared; falling below the resolution threshold within the window is read as compatible with E, and non-convergence within the window does not breach E (counterexample F21-7); this model makes no prediction about long-run convergence. If a contraction null hypothesis E\_c is set up separately (12.8), it is tested by its distance, threshold and window declared in advance, and the result concerns only E\_c. O′ (unique global optimum) predicts order independence; M (temporal memory) predicts that the old path is preferentially reoccupied rather than disappearing through burial by low flows.  
P21-2  Burial erasure and symmetrization (with amplitude, state, subsequent driving and error stated)  
　Antecedents: the low-flow segment is long enough that both mouths are buried by footslope deposits to the same elevation (confirmed by scanning that the two mouth profiles agree within the measurement error e\_s). Reading map as in P21-1.  
　Prediction: (1) After this, path selection no longer favors the old mouth. (2) On a state with identical mouth profiles, deepen one mouth artificially by δ\_obs (δ\_obs must be much larger than e\_s), then apply n high flows (n increasing as δ\_obs decreases); the main pathway then falls on the deepened mouth. Model reference: on the symmetric state, adding δ = 1/128, 1/32, 1/8, 1/4 (model units) to a gully mouth requires 13, 7, 3 and 1 high flows respectively before Dom\_8 first holds (C7g′); when δ is smaller than the measurement error of the profile difference, this prediction makes no adjudication (an initial difference within the error can point to either mouth).  
　Breach criterion: after burial has been confirmed (profile difference \< e\_s), the old mouth is still preferentially occupied, with incomplete burial and grain-size differences of the fill excluded.  
　Point of distinction: M (temporal memory: erased only after n steps without a visit) predicts that when the mouth is buried but the time has not yet elapsed, the old mouth is still preferentially occupied; this prediction holds that filling alone erases. Healing-type memory rules (filling changes memory) point in the same direction as this prediction, so a distinction must be set up separately.  
P21-3  Time of law generation and depth of the old Trace-Response  
　Antecedents as in P21-1, varying the initial depth δ of the old mouth. Prediction: the number of high flows needed before the main pathway first carries the main throughflow for both high and low flows increases as δ decreases (a prediction of the time of the immediate main relation; in the model, the first dominance time recomputed with the same Dom\_8 coincides with it, C7g′). Breach: the number needed is independent of δ.  
　Historical localization (P21-3′): truncation–challenge protocol: after the first k high flows, apply the same low-flow challenge segment and measure whether the main pathway carries the main throughflow throughout the challenge; the model predicts a threshold k\* such that it bears for k ≥ k\* and does not for k \< k\* (model k\* = 4, C7h). Shift protocol: with totals unchanged, move j low flows ahead of the high-flow segment; the model predicts a j\* such that for j ≥ j\* a main pathway no longer forms (model j\* = 4, C7i). These two protocols serve to localize which segment of history is responsible, not merely to show that order can make a difference.  
P21-4  Negative write-back thresholds: backwater length, wave reworking, flood-season lake level  
　Antecedents: the same high-flow-first history, varying separately the backwater length (lake-basin water depth or gradient), lakeshore reworking (wave-generation intensity), and the phase of lake level relative to inflow. Prediction: when backwater length shortens, reworking weakens or the lake level is high in the flood season, the main pathway forms temporarily and is then lost, or does not form (5.6.3); treating the lake-mouth sand bar can stabilize a main pathway that otherwise could not become stable (9.1.2); the treatments must be carried out in three kinds, with absolute sand amounts reported separately: periodic removal of the sand bar, continuous prevention of bar deposition with the sand allowed into the lake, and continuous prevention with export. The two claims are separate, and each is breachable:  
　P21-4a (treatment can stabilize the main pathway): under the condition that the lake level does not vary with lake-entering sand (clamped, or a sufficiently large lake basin) and backwater is moderate, all three treatments make a main pathway that otherwise could not become stable gain candidate dominance (𝔐21: at the end of macro-step 3 under Lb = 1, C8d). A second statement, listed apart from the above: under the condition that the lake level feeds back on lake-entering sand and outflow capacity is low, all three treatments delay the opening of the new channel (𝔐21⁺: from event 13 to event 16, X5b); this is a reading of the opening time, not dominance under perturbation on 𝔐21⁺. Breach: no treatment changes the stability or the opening time of the main pathway. Point of distinction: if stability were determined only by inflow statistics (E), the treatments would have no effect.  
　P21-4b (equivalence of the three treatments): holds only without lake-level feedback, and only for the specified readings of the tested protocol. In 𝔐21 the three give the same candidate-dominance strings and channel-exit readings (C8d–C8f), and in the clamp arms of 𝔐21⁺ the opening and event-by-event duty registration of all four arms are entirely “original path kept” (X5a; material budgets differ). With lake-level feedback they are not equivalent: the three have the same opening time (event 16), but the lake levels at the same end of observation (end of year 15) differ (rerouting to the lake highest at 0.9404, export 0.8729, periodic removal 0.8734), and the times at which the new route first bears the duty differ (rerouting to the lake at event 27, export and periodic removal at event 28) (X5c). Prediction: in a facility with a free lake level, or in a natural lake, at the same end of observation the lake level of the “sand rerouted to the lake” group is higher than those of the “export” and “periodic removal” groups, and the subsequent succession times differ. “Rises faster” is not issued: issuing it requires as antecedents a predefined rate of rise or threshold-arrival time, and readings of the lake-level time series. Breach: with a free lake level, the three groups show no systematic differences in lake level and succession time at the same end point; or, with a clamped lake level, the three groups show systematic differences in pathway outcome on the same specified readings. For periodic removal it must be stated whether the challenge follows stopped treatment or continued treatment (9.1.1); no conclusion about dominance under perturbation is drawn on 𝔐21⁺.  
　Connection with Ganti 2016 and Chadwick 2022 (to be tested): in backwater-controlled deltas avulsion nodes are set by sand deposition in the backwater; this prediction reads “no preferential reoccupation” as a conditional result of negative write-back prevailing; on slope-controlled experimental fans (where Reitz 2010 reports revisiting of old paths), negative write-back is weak, and the prediction is that main-pathway memory appears. Testable: in the same facility, change only the lake-basin depth so that backwater length varies, and see whether the reoccupation rate of old paths varies monotonically with it.  
P21-5  First-failing position  
　Antecedents: after the main pathway has formed, only low flows are supplied (high flows absent for a long time). Prediction: the flow share of the main pathway falls below the main-throughflow line after a finite number of low flows; at failure the series bottleneck reach (the downstream channel reach) shallows first, while the mouth reach is still in net scour (C6e). Breach: at failure the mouth reach is buried first while the channel reach has not shallowed (this would support “mouth-burial failure” rather than “diffusive-infilling failure”; in this model the two are switched by parameters, and adjudication requires first calibrating κ and the sediment supply of low flows).  
P21-6  Identifiability of climatic timing  
　Antecedents: a small catchment–lake-basin system, in years with similar annual precipitation totals and numbers of freeze-thaw days but different seasonal timing (or, in experiments, changing the order of the freeze-thaw and precipitation segments); hillslopes with ample sediment supply (thick regolith) and with limited sediment supply (thin regolith or bare-rock slopes) respectively. Readings: footslope deposit volume, channel-reach cross-sections, lake-basin varves (event layers or annual layers). Prediction: on hillslopes with ample sediment supply, freeze-thaw timing changes no reading (only the order of precipitation events is readable); on hillslopes with limited sediment supply, whether freeze-thaw is immediately followed by precipitation changes footslope deposits and lake-basin input; the discriminating power of lake-basin annual layers is lower than that of event layers, which is lower than that of joint readings of footslope topography and lake basin; when hillslope and lake basin are not connected, seasonal differences in low flows remain only at the footslope and do not reach the lake. Using annual layers requires first fixing annual boundaries by independent dating and confirming by genetic criteria that they are annual varves; for the field distinguishability of readings an error budget must be reported and compared with the separation margins of the model (10.2(D)). Breach: on hillslopes with ample sediment supply, freeze-thaw timing systematically changes lake-basin input (the channel by which freeze-thaw directly changes infiltration and runoff generation, which this model does not include, must be excluded, U21-5). Point of distinction: shredding theory predicts the attenuation of short-period signals in a statistical sense; this prediction gives the conditions for exact indistinguishability and the joint readings that do distinguish.  
P21-7  Test of bearing by intervention  
　Antecedents: the main pathway has formed. Protocol: separately backfill the mouth reach, backfill the downstream channel reach (to the initial surface), remove the lake-mouth sand bar, remove the footslope deposit of the opposite route, lower the mouth by deposition (capacity reduction, still passing water), and dam the mouth shut (disconnection, confirmed by flow meter to pass no water). Prediction: backfilling the mouth reach or the channel reach makes the main pathway lose dominance, which several subsequent high flows can restore; removing the sand bar or the footslope deposit of the opposite route causes no loss; capacity reduction causes loss of dominance with no short-term recovery; after the disconnection is removed, the original pathway does not recover, and the opposite route, once it has grown its own mouth bar, carries the main throughflow only for high flows (9.1.2). Breach: backfilling the channel reach does not affect the main pathway; or removing the lake-mouth sand bar destabilizes the main pathway; or the original pathway recovers immediately after the disconnection is removed.  
P21-8–P21-10  River–lake feedback and avulsion succession: see 13.3 (a priori protocols for the extended model 𝔐21⁺).  
P21-11  Failure bound on the mouth-bar difference (derived from Theorem 21-2(1); 9.3)  
　Antecedents: the main pathway has formed; reading map: m\_P − m\_Q ↔ the difference in crest height between the lake-mouth sand bars of the main pathway and the opposite route; g\_Q ↔ the conveyance capacity of the flow cross-section of the opposite route (calibrated from cross-section and gradient); Q\_S ↔ low-flow magnitude. Prediction (what follows generally): a necessary condition for the main pathway still to carry the main throughflow for low flows is that the bar-height difference is below (1 − θ)Q\_S/g\_Q (Theorem 21-2(1)). Conditional supplement: within conditions where high flows still reach the main-throughflow threshold, when the height difference crosses this bound the main pathway first loses the main throughflow for low flows; model comparison (at the end of macro-step 10 of H1, mouth bar raised to one grid step beyond the bound): high-flow share about 0.700908, duty passes; low-flow share about 0.574806, duty fails (L7b, L8f). “Low flows fail first” is issued only for this comparison or similar conditions, not as a general conclusion. Breach: after the bar-height difference crosses the bound calibrated with measured g\_Q, the main pathway still carries the main throughflow for low flows (channels not included in this model, such as mouth-bar channel splitting, must be excluded). Point of distinction: if only inflow statistics mattered (E), the bar-height difference should have no threshold effect.  
P21-12  Erasing historical increments: loss and recovery of the main throughflow, read separately from re-traversal qualification (physical-readings column of 9.2(3))  
　Antecedents: the experimental fan of P21-1; treatment: backfill the incision increments of the mouth reach and the downstream channel reach relative to a “no-inflow reference” (keeping the initial pre-cut old groove), applied before and after the main pathway stabilizes respectively, in controls with the same inflow and at the same time points. Readings: register separately the times at which each of the two inflow types (high flow, low flow) reaches the main throughflow (flow share ≥ the declared threshold θ), and the time at which re-traversal qualification read with the declared window W recovers; the observation window is declared in advance.  
　Prediction (physical readings; model correspondence limited to 𝔐21, H★, up to the end of macro-step 24): backfilling both reaches together after stabilization (end of macro-step 12 or 16 in the model) makes the readings of both inflow types immediately afterward lose the main throughflow, which recovers in later events (model recovery at the ends of macro-steps 20 and 24 respectively, L8a, L8b); backfilling only one reach allows earlier recovery (model: backfilling only the channel reach, recovery at the end of macro-step 13, L8c); backfilling both reaches together before stabilization (at every integer reading point from the end of macro-step 2 to 8 in the model) does not gain the main throughflow for both inflow types within the declared observation window (in the model, up to the end of macro-step 24) (L8d).  
　Model read-back listed separately (not a physical prediction): the structural positive end of the same R, read back with the fixed window, is not regained in either arm with both reaches backfilled up to the end of macro-step 24; in the arm that backfills only the channel reach it is regained at the end of macro-step 14 (L6a, L6b, L8c).  
　Breach (by the specified flow threshold and the observation window declared in advance): after stabilization both reaches are backfilled together and the immediately following inflows still reach the main throughflow; or the main throughflow never recovers within the declared window (for the lower bound of the window when replicating the protocol of this model, see 13.1(3)); or before stabilization both reaches are backfilled together and the main throughflow for both inflow types is gained within the declared window.  
P21-13  Brief disconnection: the relation recovers immediately, historical support lags (9.2(3))  
　Antecedents: the main pathway has formed; treatment: impose a complete blockade of the main pathway with a gate during one inflow (flow meter confirms that no water passes), then remove the gate; the inflows after gate removal follow the same subsequent sequence declared in advance. Prediction: at the first inflow after gate removal, the main pathway resumes carrying the main throughflow and sediment transport (model: disconnections at events 13 and 17; the throughflow of P during the actual disconnected event itself is zero; the capacities for both inflow types, read with normal conductance at each end of macro-step, all hold; the first inflow after gate removal is a high flow, and the duty reading holds, L8e). Breach: under the same declared subsequent inflow, the first inflow after gate removal does not recover the main throughflow or sediment transport. If recovery fails in the field, one can only go on to test whether the brief disconnection changed the structure (e.g., clogging of the mouth, incision of the opposite route); the cause cannot be pinned down from the failure of recovery alone.  
　Model window lag listed separately (a corollary at the level of reading, not a physical prediction): the re-traversal window read with the declared window W recovers only after the disconnected event has left the window; for a disconnection at event 13 (k = 12), the structural positive end of the same R exits from the end of macro-step 12 and is regained at the end of macro-step 21 (L6d, L6e).  
Test designs for instances at different scales (Column B predictions)  
　Experimental fans and flume deltas (centimeters to meters, hours): P21-1, P21-2, P21-3, P21-4 and P21-7 can all be carried out at the Caltech river–ocean facility or on similar experimental fans; the scale independence described by Paola et al. 2009 is the basis for extrapolation and also its limit (to be tested separately).  
　Delta avulsion (kilometers, years to decades): the three avulsions at the same location on the Yellow River delta (main text of Ganti 2014) can be read as the dominance of an established nodal organization; this paper predicts that after an engineered avulsion, without artificial maintenance, the river tends back toward the original nodal region, unless the new route is first amplified in a subsequent high flow (the natural counterpart of P21-1; confounding by human intervention must be declared).  
　Small catchments (hundreds of meters to kilometers, years to centuries): a fanhead trench and fan-surface deposition on an alluvial fan at the footslope stabilize, after a number of rainstorms, into one gully entering the lake; this paper predicts that such stabilization requires the seasonal order “large rainstorms before sediment-laden low flows”, and is harder on lakeshores with strongly developed lake-mouth sand bars and weak waves. Joint readings of lake varves and footslope deposits can trace back the timing of formation (P21-6).  
13.3  Breachable Predictions (Column B, a priori protocols)  
P21-8  Lake-level feedback and avulsion (clamped control)  
　Antecedents: the same lake–fan (or lake–delta) experimental facility, the same inflow and sediment-supply sequence (fixed), two groups of runs: (A) free lake level (lake water volume and lake-entering deposition can raise the lake level; the outlet is a fixed overflow weir); (B) clamped lake level (constant water level maintained by overflow and by supply and drainage). Reading map: ℓ ↔ lake-surface elevation; x\_j − c\_j ↔ thickness of in-channel aggradation in the parent channel; h0 + x\_j ↔ bankfull channel depth. “Avulsion” is registered at three observable time points (predictions and read-backs listed separately): opening (water first flows in the new channel), first majority (the new channel takes more than half the flow in one inflow), and full succession (the new channel carries both the main throughflow and the main channel-exit sediment transport). Model read-back: opening at event 13 (high flow), first majority at event 14 (low flow), full succession at event 26 (X3f). Prediction: in (A), in-channel aggradation of the parent channel grows first, and opening occurs when in-channel aggradation reaches some fraction of channel depth, and during a high-flow event; first majority comes later than opening and can occur in a subsequent low-flow event; in (B), within the same window there is no opening, or opening is markedly delayed. That opening occurs at high flow is not used to infer that first majority also occurs at high flow. Breach: no systematic difference in avulsion time and frequency between the two groups; or in (A), in-channel aggradation had not increased when the avulsion occurred. Point of distinction: if avulsion were determined only by inflow statistics (E), clamping the lake level would not change avulsion.  
P21-9  The transition period of succession and the duty that transfers first  
　Antecedents: an avulsion has been observed in P21-8 (A). Readings: the flow shares and channel-exit sediment-flux shares of the new and old channels (sediment flux at the channel outlet cross-section; if the channels differ in mouth sand-retention rate, also measure the actual lake-entering deposition flux and read it separately), measured event by event. Prediction: after opening there is a transition period in which the new channel first bears the main part of channel-exit sediment transport (model: share ≥ 0.9277; actual lake-entering share ≥ 0.927692), while its flow share has not yet reached the main-throughflow line (model: 0.4647–0.6442); during the transition period the old channel is aggrading (channel-exit sediment near 0). Breach: the new channel gains the main shares of flow and of sediment transport at the same time (no transition period); or the new channel gains flow first while sediment transport lags.  
P21-10  Conditions on lake-outlet outflow capacity and spillway sill  
　Antecedents: as in P21-8 (A), changing only the lake-outlet outflow capacity (weir width) or the spillway sill height. Prediction: lower outflow capacity or a higher sill makes avulsion earlier or makes it appear where there was none, and the final-segment lake level goes in the same direction as “succession by the new route”; with sufficiently high outflow capacity the original route is kept. Breach: avulsion time has no monotone relation to outflow capacity or sill height.  
　Note: the parameter values in all three predictions are only constructed values; numerical predictions for a real facility can be given only after calibrating the counterpart of Hs (the fraction of in-channel aggradation) and the gradient advantage of the new channel. Real landforms (lake–alluvial plain systems, reservoir backwater zones) have no measured certification yet; Column C has no certification (U21-12).

14\. Three-Column Master Table

### 14.1  Three-Column Master Table

　Column A, model column (𝔐21, model mode): the three-way split and element certificates E under the unified frame Φ8 (C1, C2); the organization hypergraph on E (C3e); Fld = 1 (C10); Lemmas 21-0 and 21-1, Theorems 21-2, 21-3, 21-4, 21-5 and 21-10(2′), Proposition 21-6, Corollary 21-6a, Theorems 21-7(B), 21-8(A), 21-9(A)(C) \[proved\]; Theorem 21-7(A)(C), the protocols of 8.4, 21-8(B)(C)(D), 21-9(B)(D), and the qualification proxy readings of 8.6 \[verified\]; formal law-formation read-back: Lemma 21-13, Propositions 21-14 and 21-15 \[proved\], Proposition 21-16 \[verified: exhaustive\], Theorem 21-17 (with declarations, 14.2); 𝔐21⁺: Proposition 21-11, Lemma 21-12 \[proved\], X0–X5 \[verified\] (Section 11).  
　Column B, prediction column: P21-1–P21-13 (Section 13, 13.3) and the test designs at three scales, all \[Prediction\], untested.  
　Column C, reality column: no measured certification; literature only to the levels checked (12.9).  
14.2  Items of Law Formation: Proved / Proxy / Semantic Condition / Gaps (issuing scope: the frozen model 𝔐21, the declarations and working domain of 8.5, the listed time points)  
　Proved (model, \[proved\]): CarrierNonInstant (Lemma 21-13); the direct dependence of DynamicRole in the generation chain (Proposition 21-14); Ω^c ⇒ segment-level ΩSupport (Proposition 21-15); the necessary condition for failure given by Theorem 21-2(1); the embedding of 𝔐21⁺ (Proposition 21-11, on the listed working domain).  
　Verified (model, \[verified\]: finite or exhaustive): initially not holding (L0a); the four sub-items of Established and their first time points (L2); the incremental contrast of DynamicRole and its same-beat write-back decomposition (L3, L3e); the three segment-level facts of ΩSupport, their first time points and per-class entry coverage (L1c, L4c, L4d); the structural positive end of the selected R first at the end of macro-step 10 (L1a); antecedents ⇒ Cap\_8: exhaustive over the prefix nodes of 𝓗\_10, pointwise recomputation for t ≥ 11, 207 states of intervention trajectories (L5a, L5c, L6f); the maintenance-free two-entry cycle and the sustained-low-flow counterexample (L5d, L5e); the three columns of interventions (L6).  
　Declared correspondences (checked on the working domain): Corr-E (with h held unchanged as the sediment-supply boundary), Corr-D, Corr-Ω (segment level; entry coverage per class).  
　Proxies (not used for formal items): DynamicRole^a, DynamicRole^h; Ω^d (counterexample F21-4); Ω^c (proved to be a stricter sufficient reading of segment-level ΩSupport); the candidate-dominance strings of Dom\_8 (Section 8).  
　Semantic condition: SC\_Hold, that the selected current capacity (Cap\_8, the two-entry cycle) corresponds to the current qualification content ReplayPotential of the source text's “holding-tendency”; it is not a physical item, is not automatically discharged by the number of re-traversal passes, and is an explicit condition.  
　Gaps: the negative end (Law = 0 for t \< 10, which must go through the necessary direction and exclude other R′); a general proof of Cap\_8 (including perturbed histories and longer histories); the formal layer of the Law Group Form (𝒮, the H\_Read group, stepwise ActArg) and execution adjudication have not been passed through (the host route does not take them as antecedents; formal-layer read-back takes the corresponding formal bridge as antecedent); the formal law formation of the N succession relation and future dominance under perturbation on 𝔐21⁺ (separate questions); measured certification (Column C).  
　Strength of conclusion: “the structural positive end of the selected R first holds at the end of macro-step 10 of H1; under the three host correspondences and the semantic carry-over of holding-tendency, Law is read back positively for 10 ≤ t ≤ 16 of H1”. If the semantic carry-over condition is not accepted, law qualification is not closed on that item.

15\. Domain of Validity  
　Model domain: 𝔐21 (two fixed routes P and Q, 8 Structural Positions, the parameters and event classes of Section 4) and 𝔐21⁺ (Section 11, adding the potential new channel N, the lake level, the lake-outlet outflow channel and clamp arms); rational parameters, finite windows, deterministic read-out and write; exact rational arithmetic and floor gridding.  
　History domain: finite sequences of events, rests and freeze-thaw beats; Dom\_N is adjudicated only on the frozen inflow class 𝓒\_K, perturbation class 𝒟 and budget b; Theorem 21-17 is issued only for the ends of macro-steps 10–16 of H1; the current capacity of 8.5(4) is issued only for the listed history family (𝓗\_10), the listed trajectories and the listed entries (BS, SB alternation).  
　Time window: topography already formed, newly added tectonic influence negligible; the formed topography is the common initial condition.  
　Verdict domain: Column A verdicts are model-mode verdicts; Column B gives conditional predictions; Column C has no certification. The read-back of Law holds only under the three host correspondences and the semantic carry-over of holding-tendency; the negative end is not adjudicated.  
　Not covered: mouth-bar channel splitting and multiple crevasses; endogenous selection of the location of the new channel, and reoccupation of old channels; continuous time and stochastic inflow; the direct effect of freeze-thaw on runoff generation, frozen-ground sealing, lake-ice push, and the full mechanism of seasonal evaporation; locality under cross-position post-processing; transfer to neural hosts.  
16\. Open Items

### 16.1  Open Items

　U21-1  Certificates for Dom\_∞ on 𝓒\_2 and above and on 𝒟₄ (wrapping effect); the ⊥ of P∞ at the end of macro-step 4.  
　U21-2  Parameter robustness: numerical conclusions are bound to the declared parameters (final-state sensitivity to the grid and the burial limit has been tested, C7f).  
　U21-3  Path generation (partial): 𝔐21⁺ contains one potential new channel N and an over-aggradation crevasse criterion (Section 11); it still does not contain multiple crevasses, mouth-bar channel splitting, endogenous selection of the location of the new channel, or reoccupation of old channels (N can be generated only once, at a preset location).  
　U21-4  Lake-basin feedback (partial): in 𝔐21⁺, lake water volume and lake-basin deposits determine the lake level and enter the reading and writing of the backwater of both routes and of lake-outlet outflow (X1, X2); in 𝔐21 the lake basin is still recorded but not read. The three sediment-destination switches have been compared in pairs on 𝔐21⁺ (X5) and are not equivalent with feedback on; challenges of dominance under perturbation on 𝔐21⁺ have no results yet.  
　U21-5  Incomplete climate mechanisms (freeze-thaw effects on runoff generation, frozen-ground sealing, lake-ice push, seasonal evaporation).  
　U21-6  Closure of the steady-state equilibrium candidate (E of 12.8); it is the antecedent for discriminating that candidate with a Discrimination Form.  
　U21-7  Dominance under continuous time and stochastic inflow (probabilistic dominance).  
　U21-8  Gridding bias: under floor, negative values of magnitude less than 16/256 are rest fixed points (Theorem 21-3(2)), which affects the retention verdict on the footslope deposit of the opposite route; general grid sensitivity has not been tested systematically.  
　U21-9  A measurable condition for “negative write-back prevails” and a proof of necessity (what 5.6.3 gives are only results at the tested parameter points).  
　U21-10  Whether the operationalized readings of 8.6 (the three sub-items of Established, the window of DynamicRole, the declared criteria for the three facts of Ω\_W) agree with the formal clauses of the Law Group Form; model-side tests of SC\_Hold are only readings, not proofs. Proxy readings are in 8.6; formal readings are in 8.5, and their host correspondences and the semantic carry-over of SC\_Hold are in 14.2.  
　U21-11  Parameter dependence of 𝔐21⁺: outcomes are adjudicated only on 8 grid points of (ko, z0) and the declared remaining parameters (X4); Hs, lenN, S0N, kV and AL have not been scanned; the forms of the crevasse criterion, the lake-outlet outflow and the gradient advantage of the new channel are representational choices (11.2).  
　U21-12  Certification on real landforms: the lake level–backwater–crevasse chain and duty succession in lake–alluvial plain systems have no measured certification yet (Column C); the time-window antecedent is that newly added tectonic influence is negligible.  
　U21-13  Scope of the evidence for current holding-tendency: antecedents ⇒ Cap\_8 is exhaustive only on the prefix nodes t = 8–10 of 𝓗\_10, and recomputed pointwise on three histories for t ≥ 11 and on 30 intervention trajectories; the maintenance-free cycle covers only the two alternating entries BS and SB; the cases of perturbed and longer histories and a general proof have not been given; the semantic carry-over of SC\_Hold is a condition; the negative end is not adjudicated; the formal law formation of the N succession relation and future dominance under perturbation on 𝔐21⁺ are separate questions.

### 16.2  Gaps

　(1) Empirical: there is no Trace-Response-level intervention (erasure, implantation, mouth plugging) and no paired run of “same cumulative totals, different order” on any real geomorphic instance; all Column B predictions await testing, and Column C has no certification. (2) Mechanism: the exponent 2 of L1, the linear backwater of L5, the sediment-supply switch of L7, the single-groove-mouth diffusion of L8, and the linear weir and crevasse criterion of 𝔐21⁺ are all representational choices, and the quantitative basis of the constitutive laws has been checked only at the level of form; for the conflict between Reitz 2010 and Ganti 2016 on reoccupation there is only a testable conditional explanation, and the conflict itself remains open. (3) Theory: Dom\_∞ has no certificate on general climate classes; the boundary of generation in parameter space has been adjudicated only on some variants, with no closed form; path generation is included only through one potential new channel at a preset location; Law is read back only conditionally and positively, and the negative end is not adjudicated. (4) Literature: the entries at the bibliographic-record and abstract levels are listed in 12.9; Jerolmack and Mohrig 2007 and Mohrig et al. 2000 were not read and are not cited.

17\. Interfaces  
17.1  To the Master Map, the Law Group Form and Later Papers  
　To *The Master Map of the Field Concretization Group Form*: Section 5 unfolds the seven steps of the concretized-field research procedure link by link on geomorphic objects; Sections 6 and 7 and Appendix A are executable models for Steps 4 to 6; 8.5 gives the format of conditional read-back for “a candidate relation gaining law qualification on a declared model” (frozen declarations, three host correspondences, two kinds of evidence for current capacity, positive and negative ends recorded separately).  
　To the Law Group Form: this paper is an instantiation (model side) of the sufficient direction of *The Necessity Form of Law* on a natural host model, carrying as conditions the three host correspondences and the semantic carry-over of SC\_Hold; the conclusions of the Law Group Form do not change thereby, nor does this paper supply the closure of H1–H3 and of the H\_Read group; Cap\_8 and the maintenance-free cycle are used as two kinds of model evidence for “a separately established current-capacity quantity”, and their semantic carry-over as ReplayPotential is a judgment that belongs to the Law Group Form. The negative end must go through the necessary direction (carrying C0 ∧ C2 ∧ H1 ∧ H2 ∧ H3) and exclude other carrying organizations; this has not yet been given.  
　Directions for later research (paper numbers not fixed): a light–gravity topic can first look for Trace-Responses according to the first link; if no change is found that is retained at the declared resolution and takes part in later action, it stops at the first link; the four-way division of this paper, “candidate dominance / structural positive end / current capacity / Law read-back”, can serve as a comparison format.  
　To \[17\] and \[18\]: only their appendix programs are invoked (Appendices A.7 and A.8; A.9 is needed for dependency closure), without modification; the requirement of Theorem 18-9 of \[18\] that “the removal response must be computed under the actual boundary conditions” is implemented in the disconnection interventions of this paper (9.1) by the declared disconnection switch.  
17.2  Shared Duty Structure (a structural comparison, not a transfer)  
　Trace-Response carriers: \[19\] KC→MBON synaptic efficacy (depression type) \| \[20\] program weights \| this paper: incision depths of gully mouth and channel reach, footslope deposit of the opposite route, mouth bar, hillslope regolith.  
　Bearing of tendency: \[19\] stored depression read out via the MBON and then returned via PPL1-α3 to rewrite other compartments \| \[20\] weights read out in the forward pass \| this paper: conductance and sill height via Kirchhoff flow division (the conductance-network form of Theorem 18-5(4) of \[18\]: the rest of the set bears it via (V\_x, R\_x)).  
　Activity-mediated writes: \[19\] writes gated by a third factor (dopamine) \| \[20\] writes driven by errors or eligibility traces \| this paper: above-threshold stream-power scour (the write amount is determined by the current flow division).  
　Positive and negative loops: \[19\] the directional law of return (continued writing and self-limiting, Theorem 19-4) \| \[20\] external-target locking and self-referential writing \| this paper: the positive gully-mouth–channel-reach loop and the negative mouth-bar loop (5.4.2).  
　Generation of law: in \[20\], the destination of external-target locking is a “law” locked by an external target; the law of this paper has no external teacher and is a selection made by self-referential writing under conservation constraints (the same duty slot as the self-referential writing family of \[20\], with a different mechanism).

### 17.3  Different Dynamic Mechanisms

　(1) Global conservation coupling: flow division is zero-sum (one route gaining water means the other losing it), whereas synaptic writes on the neural side are generally local; interventions between subsets in this paper are globally nonzero (5.3.3), close to the slime-mold tube network of 9.3 of \[18\]. (2) Mode of erasure: shallowing on the neural side is mainly by decay; this paper additionally has burial erasure: deposited sand buries the Trace-Response position by position down to the burial limit, and the erasure is exact rather than asymptotic (Theorem 21-7(B)). (2′) Separation of qualification and dominance: in the model this paper exhibits forward-looking dominance (Dom) earlier than the re-traversal historical support (ΩSupport\_W) required by the Law Group Form (8.6); whether the neural side has a similar separation is open, and no claim is made. (3) Record-type Trace-Responses: lake-basin deposits are retained but not read, with no direct counterpart on the neural side (the inference-time KV cache of \[20\] is read). (4) Masking by sand-supply saturation: reservoir saturation makes the maps of seasonal timing commute; the neural counterpart must be set up separately (does synaptic resource saturation make stimulus timing indistinguishable? This is only a question, not a claim). (5) Threshold gate: writing in this paper is a continuous above-threshold function and does not belong to the event-gated class 𝒦\_g of \[S11\]; continuous time and laws outside class F go beyond D12 of \[S2\] (5.7.1).

### 17.4  Transfer Discipline (\[S15\])

　No correspondence map Φ is declared between this paper and \[19\] or \[20\], and none of the correspondence conditions of D15-6 (laws, process tables, route chains, intervention switches) has been verified item by item. The table above is therefore only a structural comparison: it does not constitute a transfer of duty atoms and is not written up as universal identity (RB15-2: equal values not certified by a condition set are not transfer). Transferring the “order-dependent generation law” to neural hosts (for example, learning order deciding pathway selection) has three antecedents: a declared map, the verification of D15-6, and a separate proof of the erasure mechanism on the neural side (\[pending\]).

## Appendix A  Program Source

Runtime environment: Python 3.10 or later, standard library only, with exact rational arithmetic from fractions; no random numbers. The following 9 files are placed in the same directory: A.1–A.3 are the libraries of this paper, A.4–A.6 are the three run entries, and A.7–A.9 are upstream frozen source. A.7 is byte-for-byte identical to Appendix A.1 of \[17\] *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0 (main-text SHA-256 cfd8a0806c37e5a7dd27529439b6d04673a96e16530ad26951c2d198abbce355), and A.8 is byte-for-byte identical to Appendix A.1 of \[18\] *The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation* v1.0 (main-text SHA-256 b27dfc8e074d74f52fc26bf47c5d04350e1baa182dda19270905282d7e4e8272); A.9 is byte-for-byte identical to Appendix A.1 of \[S2\] *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 (main-text SHA-256 98a76c56e4fc20b8e39e9843f32e5ffca77e135886f9a809e7625f5510ff4854). A.9 is referenced by j18\_check and other functions in A.8 (dependency closure) and is not called by the programs of this paper. The three are only called, never modified. Run entry 1 checks A.7 and A.8 by SHA-256 (C0a1, C0a2). The programs read no files outside Appendix A. They give finite verification, finite exhaustive decisions, constructed witnesses and counterexamples; universal conclusions rest on the proofs in the main text.  
A.1  dm21\_model.py  Declared model 𝔐21: states, mechanism laws, the one-beat run, the main relation, interval certificates, the candidate-dominance decision, proxy readings

~~~python
"""The Mathematical Form of Field-Law Generation in Geomorphic Concretized Fields v1.0, appendix program: declared model 𝔐21 (constructed in this paper; parameters are dimensionless construction values): a two-path model of hillslope–footslope gully mouth–channel reach–lakeshore mouth bar–lake basin on pre-existing terrain.
Standard library only; every number is a fractions.Fraction; no randomness. The host interface is the same as jhc_core.Host of [17] Appendix A.1
(X, lo, hi, ground, orient, init, grid, act(w, s), comps(w, y, s)); the booking, clipping and gridding of a beat are performed by jhc_core.Host.step;
jxs_host wraps the same model as jxs_core.Host of [18] Appendix A.1, for evaluating the tendency, the action set and the affected set.
Structural Positions: for each path j ∈ {P, Q}: u_j bed incision depth at the footslope gully mouth (negative values mean footslope deposition burying the mouth); c_j incision depth of the channel reach; m_j height of the lakeshore mouth bar.
Shared positions: h hillslope regolith thickness (produced by freeze-thaw and wet-dry weathering, removed by runoff stripping); V cumulative lake-basin deposit (recorded, never read).
Beats (signals): event beat e = (Q, s_max, ℓ) (inflow, hillslope strippable limit, lake level); rest beat 'R' (dry season: diffusive infilling, lake-wave reworking, wet-dry weathering);
freeze-thaw beat 'F' (winter: freeze-thaw produces regolith; the ground is frozen and the lake ice-covered, all else unchanged).
Mechanism laws (main text 4.2 gives the source and status of each):
  L1 conductance a = g0·(h0 + u)², b = g0·(h0 + c)², in series g = ab/(a + b);
  L2 flow division (as water seeks low ground): Q_j = g_j(φ − m_j), ΣQ_j = Q, truncated to [0, Q];
  L3 excess-threshold scour (detachment-limited) E = ke·(ω − ωc)⁺, ω = Q_j·S;
  L4 excess-threshold transport capacity cap = kt·(ω − ωt)⁺ and the Exner budget: inlet Δu = min(cap − load, E); channel reach d = cap − load₁, Δc = min(E, d⁺) − λ·(−d)⁺;
  L5 backwater: S_c = (S0 − (m_j + ℓ)/Lb)⁺;
  L6 lake-mouth progradation and entry into the lake: a fraction β of the channel-exit sediment out is added to the mouth bar, and (1 − β) enters the lake basin V;
  L7 hillslope sediment supply: σ = min(h, s_max), split between the two paths by inflow share; h decreases by σ;
  L8 rest: u and c each lose a fraction κ, m loses a fraction μ, h gains wd; freeze-thaw: h gains ft.
Post-processing (position by position): clip u, c ∈ [lo, hi], m ∈ [0, mhi], h ∈ [0, hmax], V ∈ [0, Vmax]; grid floor(x·G)/G.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Fr
import jhc_core as JH
import jxs_core as JX
ZERO = Fr(0)
SITES = ('uP', 'cP', 'mP', 'uQ', 'cQ', 'mQ', 'h', 'V')
DYN = ('uP', 'cP', 'mP', 'uQ', 'cQ', 'mQ', 'h')          # positions read by the read-out law or the write law (V is recorded, never read)

def pos(x):
    return x if x > 0 else ZERO

BASE = dict(g0=Fr(1), h0=Fr(1), lo=Fr(-1, 2), hi=Fr(3), mhi=Fr(2), hmax=Fr(4), Vmax=Fr(10 ** 6),
            Se=Fr(3, 4), S0=Fr(3, 4), Lb=Fr(4), ke=Fr(1, 2), kt=Fr(2), wt=Fr(1), wc=Fr(1),
            lam=Fr(1, 2), beta=Fr(1, 2), kap=Fr(1, 16), mu=Fr(1, 4), wd=Fr(1, 64), ft=Fr(1, 8), G=256)
EVENTS = {'B': (Fr(4), Fr(1, 4), ZERO), 'S': (Fr(9, 4), Fr(3, 8), ZERO)}
INIT = {'uP': Fr(1, 4), 'cP': ZERO, 'mP': ZERO, 'uQ': ZERO, 'cQ': ZERO, 'mQ': ZERO, 'h': Fr(4), 'V': ZERO}

def pterms(p, Qj, Q, sig, m, lev):
    """Terms of one event on one path (L3–L6). Depends on the state only through (Qj, sig, m, lev).
    Inlet: load1 = min(cap, load + E), Δu = load1 − load = min(cap − load, E);
    channel reach: d = cap2 − load1, Δc = min(E2, d⁺) − λ(−d)⁺, out = load1 + Δc."""
    load = sig * Qj / Q
    om = Qj * p['Se']
    cap = p['kt'] * pos(om - p['wt'])
    E = p['ke'] * pos(om - p['wc'])
    du = min(cap - load, E)
    load1 = load + du
    Sc = pos(p['S0'] - (m + lev) / p['Lb'])
    om2 = Qj * Sc
    cap2 = p['kt'] * pos(om2 - p['wt'])
    E2 = p['ke'] * pos(om2 - p['wc'])
    d = cap2 - load1
    dc = min(E2, pos(d)) - p['lam'] * pos(-d)
    out = load1 + dc
    return dict(load=load, du=du, load1=load1, cap2=cap2, E2=E2, dc=dc, out=out,
                mouth=p['beta'] * out, basin=(1 - p['beta']) * out, Sc=Sc)

class Geo(JH.Host):
    name = 'dm21_model'
    def __init__(self, par=None, events=None, init=None):
        self.p = dict(BASE)
        if par:
            self.p.update(par)
        self.ev = dict(EVENTS if events is None else events)
        p = self.p
        self.X = list(SITES)
        self.lo = {x: ZERO for x in SITES}
        self.hi = {'h': p['hmax'], 'V': p['Vmax']}
        for j in 'PQ':
            self.lo['u' + j] = self.lo['c' + j] = p['lo']
            self.hi['u' + j] = self.hi['c' + j] = p['hi']
            self.hi['m' + j] = p['mhi']
        self.ground = {x: ZERO for x in SITES}
        self.orient = {x: 1 for x in SITES}
        self.init = dict(INIT if init is None else init)
        self.grid = p['G']
    # ---- Read-out of the tendency: L1, L2
    def seg_cond(self, z):
        return self.p['g0'] * (self.p['h0'] + z) ** 2
    def cond(self, w, j):
        """Series conductance; if path j is in the declared disconnection set closed (disconnection intervention: damming the gully mouth), the conductance is 0."""
        if j in self.p.get('closed', ()):
            return ZERO
        a, b = self.seg_cond(w['u' + j]), self.seg_cond(w['c' + j])
        return a * b / (a + b)
    def route(self, w, Q):
        gP, gQ = self.cond(w, 'P'), self.cond(w, 'Q')
        if gP == 0 or gQ == 0:
            raw = Q if gQ == 0 and gP > 0 else ZERO
            QP = raw
            return QP, Q - QP, raw
        raw = gP * (Q + gQ * (w['mQ'] - w['mP'])) / (gP + gQ)
        QP = min(Q, max(ZERO, raw))
        return QP, Q - QP, raw
    def act(self, w, s):
        """Activity: on event beats, the throughflows QP, QQ on the two paths and the channel-exit sediment transport SP, SQ into the lake; no flow on rest and freeze-thaw beats. 'rest' (the reference-history term of [17]) is the same as 'R'."""
        if s in ('R', 'F', 'rest'):
            return {'QP': ZERO, 'QQ': ZERO, 'SP': ZERO, 'SQ': ZERO}
        Q, _smax, lev = self.ev[s]
        QP, QQ, _ = self.route(w, Q)
        sig = self.supply(w, s)
        oP = pterms(self.p, QP, Q, sig, w['mP'], lev)['out']
        oQ = pterms(self.p, QQ, Q, sig, w['mQ'], lev)['out']
        return {'QP': QP, 'QQ': QQ, 'SP': oP, 'SQ': oQ}
    # ---- L3–L7: one event on one path
    def path_terms(self, w, j, Qj, Q, sig, lev):
        """Terms on one path. Where the mouth sediment goes: by default a fraction β stays on the mouth bar and (1 − β) enters the lake basin; for path P the process switches
        betaP (mouth-bar retention rate) and exportP may be declared (true: the part not retained on the bar, (β − betaP)·out, is exported from the system; false: it is reassigned to the lake basin)."""
        t = pterms(self.p, Qj, Q, sig, w['m' + j], lev)
        t['export'] = ZERO
        if j == 'P' and 'betaP' in self.p:
            bP, b = self.p['betaP'], self.p['beta']
            t['mouth'] = bP * t['out']
            if self.p.get('exportP', False):
                t['basin'] = (1 - b) * t['out']
                t['export'] = (b - bP) * t['out']
            else:
                t['basin'] = (1 - bP) * t['out']
        return t
    def supply(self, w, s):
        return min(w['h'], self.ev[s][1])
    def comps(self, w, y, s):
        p = self.p
        if s in ('R', 'rest'):
            out = []
            for j in 'PQ':
                out += [('diff', 'u' + j, -p['kap'] * w['u' + j]), ('diff', 'c' + j, -p['kap'] * w['c' + j]),
                        ('rework', 'm' + j, -p['mu'] * w['m' + j])]
            return out + [('weather', 'h', p['wd'])]
        if s == 'F':
            return [('frost', 'h', p['ft'])]
        Q, _smax, lev = self.ev[s]
        sig = self.supply(w, s)
        out = [('strip', 'h', -sig)]
        for j in 'PQ':
            t = self.path_terms(w, j, y['Q' + j], Q, sig, lev)
            out += [('scour_e', 'u' + j, t['du']), ('scour_c', 'c' + j, t['dc']),
                    ('mouth', 'm' + j, t['mouth']), ('basin', 'V', t['basin'])]
        return out
    def terms(self, w, s):
        Q, _smax, lev = self.ev[s]
        y = self.act(w, s)
        sig = self.supply(w, s)
        return sig, {j: self.path_terms(w, j, y['Q' + j], Q, sig, lev) for j in 'PQ'}, y
    def share(self, w, s):
        return self.act(w, s)['QP'] / self.ev[s][0]

def run(host, w, hist):
    return JH.run(host, w, hist)

def clampgrid(host, w):
    return {x: JH.floor_grid(min(host.hi[x], max(host.lo[x], w[x])), host.grid) for x in host.X}

def apply_dist(host, w, dist):
    """Perturbation: add {position: increment}, then clip and grid (a member of the declared perturbation class)."""
    if not dist:
        return dict(w)
    v = dict(w)
    for x, dx in dist.items():
        v[x] = v[x] + dx
    return clampgrid(host, v)

def macro(host, w, e, r, dist=None):
    """Macro-step: event beat e, then r rest beats, then the perturbation."""
    w = host.step(w, e)
    for _ in range(r):
        w = host.step(w, 'R')
    return apply_dist(host, w, dist)

def jxs_host(host):
    def read(d, r, s):
        return host.act(d, s)
    def comps(d, y, s):
        cs = {x: [] for x in host.X}
        for (p_, x, c) in host.comps(d, y, s):
            cs[x].append((p_, c))
        return cs
    def post(dn, do):
        return clampgrid(host, dn)
    return JX.Host(host.X, read, comps, post)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Relations (candidate laws)
def rel_water(theta, j='P'):
    def rel(host, w, e):
        return host.act(w, e)['Q' + j] >= theta * host.ev[e][0]
    return rel

def rel_main(theta, j='P'):
    """Main relation R*_θ(j): on event beats the path carries the main throughflow (share ≥ θ) and the main sediment delivery to the lake (channel-exit sediment > 0 and share ≥ θ)."""
    o = 'Q' if j == 'P' else 'P'
    def rel(host, w, e):
        _sig, t, y = host.terms(w, e)
        if y['Q' + j] < theta * host.ev[e][0]:
            return False
        a, b = t[j]['out'], t[o]['out']
        return a > 0 and a >= theta * (a + b)
    return rel

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Interval arithmetic (exact rational endpoints; used for invariant-box certificates)
class I:
    __slots__ = ('a', 'b')
    def __init__(self, a, b=None):
        self.a = Fr(a)
        self.b = Fr(a if b is None else b)
        assert self.a <= self.b
    def __add__(s, o):
        o = o if isinstance(o, I) else I(o)
        return I(s.a + o.a, s.b + o.b)
    __radd__ = __add__
    def __neg__(s):
        return I(-s.b, -s.a)
    def __sub__(s, o):
        o = o if isinstance(o, I) else I(o)
        return I(s.a - o.b, s.b - o.a)
    def __rsub__(s, o):
        return I(o) - s
    def __mul__(s, o):
        o = o if isinstance(o, I) else I(o)
        ps = (s.a * o.a, s.a * o.b, s.b * o.a, s.b * o.b)
        return I(min(ps), max(ps))
    __rmul__ = __mul__
    def inv(s):
        assert s.a > 0
        return I(1 / s.b, 1 / s.a)
    def __truediv__(s, o):
        o = o if isinstance(o, I) else I(o)
        return s * o.inv()
    def __repr__(s):
        return '[%s, %s]' % (JH.fstr(s.a), JH.fstr(s.b))

def imin(x, y):
    return I(min(x.a, y.a), min(x.b, y.b))

def imax(x, y):
    return I(max(x.a, y.a), max(x.b, y.b))

def ipos(x):
    return I(pos(x.a), pos(x.b))

def isq_shift(h0, z):
    """(h0 + z)², z an interval with h0 + z > 0 (monotonically increasing)."""
    lo, hi = h0 + z.a, h0 + z.b
    assert lo > 0
    return I(lo * lo, hi * hi)

def box_route(host, B, Q):
    """Interval flow division: share s0 = 1/(1 + gQ/gP), parallel term 1/(1/gP + 1/gQ)·Δm; every quantity is written in monotone form, each variable appearing only once."""
    p = host.p
    def g(j):
        a = p['g0'] * isq_shift(p['h0'], B['u' + j])
        b = p['g0'] * isq_shift(p['h0'], B['c' + j])
        return (a.inv() + b.inv()).inv()
    gP, gQ = g('P'), g('Q')
    s0 = (I(1) + gQ / gP).inv()
    par = (gP.inv() + gQ.inv()).inv()
    raw = Q * s0 + par * (B['mQ'] - B['mP'])
    QP = imin(I(Q), imax(I(0), raw))
    return QP, I(Q) - QP

def box_terms(host, B, Qj, Q, sig, lev, j):
    """Interval terms on one path. out is nondecreasing in Qj, nondecreasing in sig and nonincreasing in m (proof in main text 7.2, Lemma 21-0), so its corner bounds are exact;
    Δu takes the natural interval extension; Δc is nonincreasing in load1 and nondecreasing in cap2 and E2, each of the three taking its monotone corner bound. All are sound envelopes."""
    p = host.p
    m = B['m' + j]
    lo = pterms(p, Qj.a, Q, sig.a, m.b, lev)
    hi = pterms(p, Qj.b, Q, sig.b, m.a, lev)
    out = I(lo['out'], hi['out'])
    load = sig * Qj / Q
    om = Qj * p['Se']
    cap = p['kt'] * ipos(om - p['wt'])
    E = p['ke'] * ipos(om - p['wc'])
    du = imin(cap - load, E)
    l1a, l1b = lo['load1'], hi['load1']
    c2a, c2b = lo['cap2'], hi['cap2']
    e2a, e2b = lo['E2'], hi['E2']
    def g(l1, c2, e2):
        dd = c2 - l1
        return min(e2, pos(dd)) - p['lam'] * pos(-dd)
    dc = I(g(l1b, c2a, e2a), g(l1a, c2b, e2b))
    return du, dc, out

def box_step(host, B, s):
    """Interval image of one beat (including clipping and gridding, both monotone). B: {position: I} (DYN only)."""
    p = host.p
    N = {x: B[x] for x in DYN}
    if s == 'R':
        for j in 'PQ':
            N['u' + j] = B['u' + j] * (1 - p['kap'])
            N['c' + j] = B['c' + j] * (1 - p['kap'])
            N['m' + j] = B['m' + j] * (1 - p['mu'])
        N['h'] = B['h'] + p['wd']
    elif s == 'F':
        N['h'] = B['h'] + p['ft']
    else:
        Q, smax, lev = host.ev[s]
        sig = imin(B['h'], I(smax))
        QP, QQ = box_route(host, B, Q)
        N['h'] = B['h'] - sig
        for j, Qj in (('P', QP), ('Q', QQ)):
            du, dc, out = box_terms(host, B, Qj, Q, sig, lev, j)
            N['u' + j] = B['u' + j] + du
            N['c' + j] = B['c' + j] + dc
            N['m' + j] = B['m' + j] + p['beta'] * out
    for x in DYN:
        lo, hi = host.lo[x], host.hi[x]
        a = JH.floor_grid(min(hi, max(lo, N[x].a)), host.grid)
        b = JH.floor_grid(min(hi, max(lo, N[x].b)), host.grid)
        N[x] = I(a, b)
    return N

def box_rel_main(host, B, e, theta):
    """Sufficient check that the main relation holds at every point of box B: lower bound of the throughflow share; for sediment, the lower bound of oP and the upper bound of oQ (taking the lower end of QP and the corner bounds of out)."""
    p = host.p
    Q, smax, lev = host.ev[e]
    QP, QQ = box_route(host, B, Q)
    if QP.a < theta * Q:
        return False
    sa, sb = min(B['h'].a, smax), min(B['h'].b, smax)
    oP = pterms(p, QP.a, Q, sa, B['mP'].b, lev)['out']
    oQ = pterms(p, Q - QP.a, Q, sb, B['mQ'].a, lev)['out']
    return oP > 0 and (1 - theta) * oP - theta * oQ >= 0

def split(B, k):
    """Bisect the box along its widest dimension k times and return the list of sub-boxes (their union equals the original box)."""
    out = [B]
    for _ in range(k):
        nxt = []
        for b in out:
            x = max(DYN, key=lambda z: b[z].b - b[z].a)
            iv = b[x]
            if iv.b - iv.a <= 0:
                nxt.append(b)
                continue
            mid = (iv.a + iv.b) / 2
            b1, b2 = dict(b), dict(b)
            b1[x], b2[x] = I(iv.a, mid), I(mid, iv.b)
            nxt += [b1, b2]
        out = nxt
    return out

def box_macro(host, B, e, r, dist, k):
    """Interval image of a macro-step: subdivide B k times, take the image of each sub-box, then the envelope."""
    acc = None
    for b in split(B, k):
        b = box_step(host, b, e)
        for _ in range(r):
            b = box_step(host, b, 'R')
        if dist:
            b = dict(b)
            for x, dx in dist.items():
                lo, hi = host.lo[x], host.hi[x]
                b[x] = I(JH.floor_grid(min(hi, max(lo, b[x].a + dx)), host.grid),
                         JH.floor_grid(min(hi, max(lo, b[x].b + dx)), host.grid))
        acc = b if acc is None else {x: I(min(acc[x].a, b[x].a), max(acc[x].b, b[x].b)) for x in DYN}
    return acc

def hull_pts(pts):
    return {x: I(min(w[x] for w in pts), max(w[x] for w in pts)) for x in DYN}

def contains(B, C):
    return all(B[x].a <= C[x].a and C[x].b <= B[x].b for x in DYN)

def box_cert(host, w0, theta, macros, k=3, seeds=(), iters=60):
    """Invariant-box certificate. macros: the frozen class of macro-events [(steps, label)], steps = [(e, r, perturbation), …] (one block).
    Starting from the envelope of w0 and the seed points, iterate B ← hull(B ∪ interval images of the blocks) to a fixed point; on the intermediate box before
    each event within a block, check that the main relation holds at every point (interval lower bound, k subdivisions). Return ('1', B, rounds) or ('⊥', B, rounds)."""
    B = hull_pts([w0] + list(seeds))
    for it in range(iters):
        imgs = []
        for (steps, _lab) in macros:
            C = B
            for (e, r, dist) in steps:
                for b in split(C, k):
                    if not box_rel_main(host, b, e, theta):
                        return '⊥', B, it
                C = box_macro(host, C, e, r, dist, k)
            imgs.append(C)
        if all(contains(B, C) for C in imgs):
            return '1', B, it
        for C in imgs:
            B = {x: I(min(B[x].a, C[x].a), max(B[x].b, C[x].b)) for x in DYN}
    return '⊥', B, iters

def refute(host, w0, rel, macros, depth=6, limit=20000):
    """Finite-depth counterexample search: expand the frozen block class layer by layer (deduplicated); if the relation fails before some event within a block, return the counterexample sequence."""
    key = lambda w: tuple(w[x] for x in DYN)
    layer = [(w0, [])]
    seen = {key(w0)}
    n = 0
    for d in range(depth):
        nxt = []
        for w, path in layer:
            for (steps, lab) in macros:
                x, sub = w, []
                bad = None
                for (e, r, dist) in steps:
                    if not rel(host, x, e):
                        bad = path + [lab, ('in-block read', e, len(sub))]
                        break
                    x = macro(host, x, e, r, dist)
                    sub.append(e)
                if bad:
                    return bad
                k2 = key(x)
                if k2 in seen:
                    continue
                seen.add(k2)
                n += 1
                if n > limit:
                    return None
                nxt.append((x, path + [lab]))
        layer = nxt
    return None

def dominion(host, w0, theta, macros, k=3, seeds=(), depth=4):
    """Dominance judgment for the law (Definition 21-4): '0' gives a counterexample; '1' the invariant-box certificate holds; '⊥' neither was obtained."""
    path = refute(host, w0, rel_main(theta), macros, depth)
    if path is not None:
        return '0', path
    v, B, it = box_cert(host, w0, theta, macros, k, seeds)
    return (v, B) if v == '1' else ('⊥', B)

def regime(K, r, dists, big='B', small='S'):
    """Climate class 𝓒_K: at least one high flow in every K events; generated by the blocks small^j big (0 ≤ j ≤ K − 1), with one perturbation allowed at the end of a block."""
    out = []
    for j in range(K):
        for dn, dv in dists:
            steps = [(small, r, None)] * j + [(big, r, dv)]
            out.append((steps, (small * j + big, dn)))
    return out

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Finite-horizon dominance Dom_N (exhaustive, exactly decidable)
def dom_N(host, w0, theta, K, r, dists, N, budget=1, big='B', small='S', limit=400000, c0=0, rel=None, mac=None):
    """Dom_N (Definition 21-4(A)): climate class 𝓒_K (at most K − 1 consecutive low flows) × r rest beats × perturbation class (the non-identity members of dists,
    applied at most budget times within the window, each after some event). The main relation is read at every step on all admissible sequences of length N;
    the enumeration is exhaustive after deduplication by (state, count of consecutive low flows, remaining perturbations). Return ('1'|'0'|'⊥', number visited, counterexample or None).
    Number and order of reads (Definition 21-4(A)): one read before each of events 1 to N + 1 (N + 1 reads in all; the (N + 1)-th read applies no
    event); a macro-step runs in the order “read → event beat → r rest beats → at most one perturbation” (mac may declare another macro-step, such as the “continued-treatment” macro-step);
    rel defaults to the main relation rel_main(θ) and may declare another target quantity (such as the actual sediment delivery to the lake). Each verdict restarts from w0:
    the count of consecutive low flows starts at c0 (default 0) and the perturbation budget at budget; counts and perturbations used in the actual history are not inherited."""
    rel = rel_main(theta) if rel is None else rel
    mac = macro if mac is None else mac
    key = lambda w, c, b: tuple(w[x] for x in DYN) + (c, b)
    layer = {key(w0, c0, budget): (w0, c0, budget, [])}
    n = 0
    for step in range(N + 1):
        nxt = {}
        for (w, c, b, path) in layer.values():
            opts = [big] + ([small] if c < K - 1 else [])
            for e in opts:
                if not rel(host, w, e):
                    return '0', n, path + [('read', e)]
                if step == N:
                    continue
                for dn, dv in dists:
                    if dv is not None and b == 0:
                        continue
                    w2 = mac(host, w, e, r, dv)
                    c2 = 0 if e == big else c + 1
                    b2 = b - (dv is not None)
                    k2 = key(w2, c2, b2)
                    if k2 not in nxt:
                        nxt[k2] = (w2, c2, b2, path + [(e, dn)])
                        n += 1
                        if n > limit:
                            return '⊥', n, None
        layer = nxt
    return '1', n, None

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- Gully-depth proxy reading of Law Group Form qualification (main text 8.6; used for counterexample F21-4)
def replay_hist(host, macro_states, macro_events, t, W, theta, conds=('B', 'S'), nmin=2, halves=2):
    """Gully-depth proxy reading Ω^d of Ω_W (connection read as min(u_P, c_P) > 0; main text 8.6. This reading is distorted under disconnection; see counterexample F21-4).
    macro_states[k]: the state before the k-th macro-step (k = 0 … ); macro_events[k]: the event of the k-th macro-step. The window W consists of the macro-steps [t − W, t) ending at t.
    For each applicable condition c: (1) the events in the window of type c at which the main relation holds when read (re-traversal records) number at least nmin;
    (2) Brk_W = 0: before every macro-step in the window and at t, the incision depths of the gully mouth and of the channel reach on path P are both > 0 (the Gully-Line connection being followed is maintained);
    (3) the window is divided into halves equal segments, and every segment has a re-traversal record of c. Coverage polarity: 1 only if all of this holds for every c in conds."""
    if t < W:
        return 0, {}
    rel = rel_main(theta)
    ks = list(range(t - W, t))
    rec = {c: [k for k in ks if macro_events[k] == c and rel(host, macro_states[k], c)] for c in conds}
    brk = any(min(macro_states[k]['uP'], macro_states[k]['cP']) <= 0 for k in ks + [t])
    seg = W // halves
    out = {}
    for c in conds:
        f1 = len(rec[c]) >= nmin
        f3 = all(any(t - W + i * seg <= k < t - W + (i + 1) * seg for k in rec[c]) for i in range(halves))
        out[c] = (f1, not brk, f3)
    v = int(all(all(x) for x in out.values()))
    return v, out

def element_cert(host, w_frame, x, sign, probe, readout, wcond, tauA, tauW, eps, ref=None, dom=None):
    """Element certificate under the same frame for Definitions 2′, 3′, 4′ of [17]: the three-way split is taken from the supply cut-off rest from w_frame (as a difference against ref when a reference is given);
    K1 holds by construction; K2 and K3 are enumerated exhaustively, one value at a time, over the own-position grid domain with the other carriers in the trace-free ground state (relative to the probe probe, read-out readout and write condition wcond).
    dom: the own-position grid domain (default: the clipping interval; the clipping bound of V is a declared large upper bound, so a finite declared domain is used). Return dict(lower bound, verdict, three-way split, K2, K3, certificate)."""
    w = dict(w_frame)
    wr = dict(ref) if ref is not None else None
    seq = []
    for t in range(400):
        seq.append(w[x] - (wr[x] if wr is not None else host.ground[x]))
        w = host.step(w, 'R')
        if wr is not None:
            wr = host.step(wr, 'R')
    lam = host.p['mu'] if x[0] == 'm' else host.p['kap']
    if x in ('h', 'V'):
        lb, st = JH.retention(seq, eps, ('coverage',))
    else:
        stp = lambda v: JH.floor_grid(v * (1 - lam), host.grid)
        band = [Fr(i, host.grid) for i in range(-2, 3) if abs(Fr(i, host.grid)) <= eps]
        lb, st = JH.retention(seq, eps, ('invariant set', stp, band))
    tri = JH.triage(lb, st, tauA, tauW)
    gnd = {y: ZERO for y in SITES}
    e0 = host.act(gnd, probe)[readout]
    lo, hi = (host.lo[x], host.hi[x]) if dom is None else dom
    vals = [Fr(i, host.grid) for i in range(int(lo * host.grid), int(hi * host.grid) + 1)]
    vals = [v for v in vals if (v > 0 if sign > 0 else v < 0)]
    pairs, k3 = [], True
    for v in vals:
        ww = dict(gnd)
        ww[x] = v
~~~
